L331: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L332: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L333: Inotuzumab ozogamicin is an ADC consisting of a humanized monoclonal antibody targeting CD22+ B-cells linked N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin, which binds to the DNA minor groove and induces DNA double strand breakage and cell death. The proposed indication for inotuzumab ozogamicin is the treatment of relapsed or refractory B-cell precursor acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL).
L334: In the phase 3 trial, the CR/CRi rate was 73.2% (95% CI: 65.7-79.8%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm vs. 30.9% (95% CI: 23.9-38.6%) in the control arm (investigator's choice of chemotherapy). Inotuzumab did not show a statistically significant survival advantage; the hazard ratio for overall survival was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.578 - 1.026).
L335: A major focus of this review was the appropriateness of the proposed dose of 1.8 mg/m2. This dose was identified as the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) in the dose finding trials. The major safety concern for this drug is VOD. The rate of VOD in patients treated with inotuzumab ozogamicin was 13.4% compared to a rate of 0.1% in patients treated with chemotherapy. In addition, the rate of VOD in patients who proceeded to hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HSCT) was even higher; 22.1% in the inotuzumab arm compared to 3.1% in the control arm. Inotuzumab ozogamicin appears to amplify the VOD properties of the preparatory regimen (chemotherapy + radiation) that is administered prior to HSCT.
L336: Exposure-response relationship for efficacy (CR/CRi) and safety (VOD) were observed at the proposed 1.8 mg/m2 dose. The dose escalation study evaluated a narrow range of inotuzumab doses (1.2 mg/m2, 1.6 mg/m2, and 1.8 mg/m2) and enrolled limited number of patients (N=3) at the lowest dose. Therefore, a dose finding PMR will be issued for the following reasons:
L337: - Dose finding trial was not robust and didn't identify the optimal dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin that balances safety and efficacy.
- Inotuzumab ozogamicin caused very high rate of VOD, including mortalities.
- Prior experience with calicheamicin containing antibody (gemtuzumab ozogamicin) showed very high rate of VOD post approval.
L338: ## 6.1.1. Recommendations
L339: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in BLA 761040. This BLA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations are summarized below:
L340: Review Issues	Recommendations and Comments
Supportive Evidence of Effectiveness	Evidence of effectiveness was obtained from the pivotal Phase 3 trial B1931022 and the supportive Phase 1/2 trial B1931010. The exposure-response analysis identified a correlation between
L341: exposure and probability of achieving CR/CRi. The analysis was conducted with data primarily from one dose level and thus needs to be interpreted with caution.
General Dosing Instructions	The recommended dosing for inotuzumab ozogamicin during the first cycle of treatment is 1.8 mg/m2 divided into three weekly doses of 0.8 mg/m2 on Day 1, 0.5 mg/m2 on Day 8, and 0.5 mg/m2 on Day 15 of a 21-day-cycle. In subsequent cycles, three doses of 0.5 mg/m2 are given on Days 1, 8, and 15 of a 28-day-cycle.
Dosing in Patient Subgroups (Intrinsic and Extrinsic Factors)	No dose adjustments are proposed based on intrinsic factors or extrinsic factors.
Labeling	Labeling recommendations are generally adequate.
Bridge between the "to-be-marketed" and clinical trial formulation	The to-be-marketed formulation was used in a subset of patients in the registration trial. Additionally, analytical comparability studies between the to-be-marketed formulation and the clinical trial formulation was established.
L342: ## 6.1.2. Post-Marketing Requirements and Commitments
L343: PMR or PMC	Key Issues to be Addressed	Rationale	Key Considerations for Design Features
PMR	Dose Selection	The proposed inotuzumab ozogamicin dose of 1.8 mg/m2 may not be optimal in balancing safety and efficacy. The relatively high rate of VOD adverse events is a significant concern.
(b) (4)
was not informed by a robust dose finding study, a lower dose may provide a better balance of safety and efficacy.	Conduct a randomized trial of at least 2 dose levels of inotuzumab ozogamicin in patients with relapsed or refractory acute lymphoblastic leukemia who are potential candidates of HSCT and at high risk for developing veno-occlusive disease (VOD). High risk is defined as patients with prior hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT), ongoing or prior liver disease, older patients (\ge 55 years), or later salvage line (Salvage \ge 2). Safety parameters will include hepatic VOD, transplant related mortality (non-relapse mortality), and non-transplant related mortality. Descriptive analyses of safety and efficacy
L344: (including achievement of minimal residual disease [MRD]-negativity) will be conducted for the intent-to-treat population and the per-protocol population that excludes patients who do not proceed to HSCT. The study will include sufficient clinical pharmacokinetic sampling to analyze the exposure-response relationship for efficacy and safety. Submit complete clinical study report and datasets.
L345: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L346: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L347: **Mechanism of Action:** inotuzumab ozogamicin consists of a humanized IgG4 monoclonal antibody targeting CD22+ B-cells and N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin, which induces DNA double-strand breaks resulting in cell cycle arrest and apoptosis.
L348: **Absorption:** inotuzumab ozogamicin is administered as a 1- hour IV infusion.
L349: **Distribution:** inotuzumab ozogamicin volume of distribution is approximately 12 liters, based on population PK analysis. Unconjugated calicheamicin is ~97% bound to plasma proteins and exhibits limited partitioning into red blood cells.
L350: **Metabolism:** N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin is the only metabolite detected in plasma and is produced non-enzymatically by hydrolytic release. CYP450 enzymes isoforms and UGT enzymes are not involved in the metabolism of calicheamicin.
L351: **Elimination:** Non-clinical studies suggest that biliary excretion is the primary route of calicheamicin elimination and renal excretion exhibit minor involvement (8-9%).
L352: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L353: ## General Dosing
L354: The recommended dosing for inotuzumab ozogamicin during the first cycle of treatment is 1.8 mg/m2 divided into three weekly doses of 0.8 mg/m2 on Day 1, 0.5 mg/m2 on Day 8, and 0.5 mg/m2 on Day 15. The duration of the first cycle is 21 days and can be extended to 28 days for patients who achieve CR/CRI or to allow for recovery from toxicity.
L355: Subsequent treatment cycles are 28 days. The recommended dosing is 1.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 divided into 3 weekly doses of 0.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 on Day 1, Day 8, and Day 15 for patients who achieve CR/CRi. The recommended dosing for patients who do not achieve CR/CRi is 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 divided into 3 weekly doses of 0.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 on Day 1, 0.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 on Day 8, and 0.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 on Day 15.
L356: For patients proceeding to hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HSCT), the recommended duration of treatment is 2 cycles. A third cycle should be considered for patients who do not achieve CR/CRi and minimal residual disease (MRD) negativity after 2 cycles.
L357: For patients not proceeding to HSCT (b) (4) up to 6 cycles of treatment may be administered. Patients who do not achieve a CR/CRi within 3 cycles of treatment should discontinue treatment.
L358: Due to lack of robust dose finding data and very high rate of VOD, a PMR will be issued in order to identify an optimal dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin.
L359: ## Therapeutic Individualization
L360: No dose adjustments are proposed based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors. Based on population PK analyses, inotuzumab ozogamicin PK was not affected by mild, moderate or severe renal and hepatic impairment. Of note, only one patient had severe hepatic impairment. In addition, age, race, or sex did not influence the PK of inotuzumab ozogamicin. *In vitro* studies suggest that inotuzumab ozogamicin exhibits low potential for drug-drug interactions.
L361: ## Outstanding Issues
L362: The applicant selected the maximum tolerated dose of 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 for the pivotal efficacy study. At this dose, the rate of VOD is substantially higher than the control arm, especially in patients proceeding to HSCT. Since the dose escalation study enrolled very few patients at doses lower than 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2, robust dose or exposure-response data were not available to enable optimal dose selection. Based on available data, we cannot determine whether 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 of inotuzumab ozogamicin sufficiently balances safety and efficacy.
L363: Prior experience with gemtuzumab ozogamicin demonstrated that the rate of VOD decreased substantially with dose reduction. Therefore, the applicant will be asked to conduct a post-marketing dose finding study to assess the safety and efficacy of lower inotuzumab ozogamicin doses.
L364: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L365: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L366: PHARMACOLOGY
Structure	Inotuzumab ozogamicin is a CD22-targeted antibody-drug conjugate. The antibody is a humanized immunoglobulin type G, which specifically recognizes human CD22. The small molecule, N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamycin
L367: dimethyl hydrazide (DMH), is a deoxyribonucleic acid (DNA) minor groove binding cytotoxic natural product that induces double strand DNA breaks. N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin DMH is covalently attached to the antibody via a linker.
Mechanism of Action	The anticancer activity of inotuzumab ozogamicin is due to the binding of the ADC to CD22 expressing cells, followed by internalization of the ADC-CD22 complex, and the release of N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin DMH via hydrolytic cleavage in the acidic environment in lysosomes. Binding of N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin DMH to DNA leads to DNA strand breakage, subsequently inducing cell cycle arrest and apoptosis.
QT Prolongation	
In the pivotal phase 3 study, increase in QTcF of \ge 60 msec from baseline were measured in 4/162 (3%) patients in the inotuzumab arm compared to 3/124 (2%) patients in the investigator's choice arm. Increases in QTcF of > 500 msec were not observed in the inotuzumab arm.
Central tendency analysis of the QTcF interval changes from baseline showed that the highest mean (upper bound of the 2-sided 90% CI) for QTcF was 15.3 (21.1) msec, which was observed at cycle 4/Day 1/1 hour in the BESPONSA arm.

GENERAL INFORMATION
Bioanalysis	
ALL Patients (Study B1931010 and B1931022)
Plasma concentrations of inotuzumab ozogamicin and unconjugated calicheamicin were measured using a validated high performance liquid chromatography tandem mass spectrometry assay (HPLC/MS-MS).
NHL Patients (9 Studies)
ELISA methods were used to measure the serum PK concentrations of inotuzumab ozogamicin, total calicheamicin, unconjugated calicheamicin, and total antibody.
Immunogenicity
In patients with relapsed or refractory ALL, ECL and cell-based assays were used to detect ADA and nAb to inotuzumab ozogamicin in human serum.

Dose Proportionality	
Single doses ranging from 0.4, 0.8, 1.34, 1.8, and 2.4 mg/m2 were studied in patients with NHL. The CMAX of inotuzumab ozogamicin appeared to increase in a less-than-proportional manner with increasing dose (logarithmic slope = 0.58); however, the 90% confidence interval included zero. Similar trend was observed for total calicheamicin CMAX.
The AUCINF of total calicheamicin increased in a more-than-dose proportional manner with increasing dose (logarithmic slope = 1.32); however, the 90% confidence interval included zero.

Accumulation	At the proposed dosing regimen, the accumulation ratio (total AUC at Cycle 4 versus total AUC at Cycle 1) in patients with relapsed or refractory ALL was
L368: estimated to be 5.30.
The unconjugated calicheamicin concentrations in serum at pre-dose and end of infusion were below the limit of quantitation for most of the samples.
Variability	Pharmacokinetic data obtained from the pivotal study show that inotuzumab ozogamicin exhibits high variability. The %CV for C_{MAX} and C_{TROUGH} ranged 44% to 139% and 42% to 301%, respectively. Based on the population PK modeling estimates, the variability for cumulative AUC ranged from 53% to 79%.
Immunogenicity	In clinical studies of inotuzumab ozogamicin in patients with relapsed or refractory ALL, 7/236 (3%) patients tested positive for anti-inotuzumab ozogamicin antibodies.
In patients testing positive for anti-drug antibodies (ADA), neutralizing antibodies (nAbs) were not detected. The CL of inotuzumab ozogamicin appeared to be comparable between patients who tested positive or negative for ADAs.
DISTRIBUTION
Volume of Distribution	Based on population PK analysis, the total volume of distribution was approximately 12 L.
Plasma Protein Binding	Unconjugated calicheamicin was highly bound to plasma proteins (97.2%).
Blood-to-Plasma & Tissue-to-plasma ratio	In preclinical studies, inotuzumab ozogamicin exhibited limited distribution into tissues, with tissue-to-plasma ratio < 0.6. Additionally, limited distribution to red blood cells was observed in rats. The blood-to-plasma ratio was approximately 0.5.
ELIMINATION
Mean Terminal Half-Life	The mean terminal half-life of inotuzumab ozogamicin after Cycle 1 Day 1 dose was 144 hours (6 days) and 329 hours (13.7 days) by the end of Cycle 4. Inotuzumab ozogamicin clearance is influenced by the interaction with CD22-positive B-cells and binding with neonatal Fc receptor (FcRN). The observed increase in half-life is attributed to the depletion of the target CD22-positive B-cells
METABOLISM
Primary Metabolic Pathways	In the serum of patients who were treated with inotuzumab ozogamicin, the only metabolite detected was N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin DMH. The hydrolytic release of N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin from the ADC in plasma is limited and the metabolism of N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin DMH occurs primarily via non-enzymatic reduction.
Transporter Substrate	Bidirectional permeability assays in MDCK cells demonstrated that unconjugated calicheamicin is substrate for the P-gp efflux transporter but
L369: not BCRP.
In vitro studies in hepatocytes demonstrated that unconjugated calicheamicin was not a substrate of the hepatic uptake transporters OATP1B1 or OATP1B3.

DDI Potential	
CYP450 Inhibition
In vitro studies demonstrated that calicheamicin inhibited CYP450 isozymes (CYP1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, and 3A4) with IC50 values ranging from 0.42 to >10 mM, which are orders of magnitude higher than clinical concentrations. Inotuzumab ozogamicin IC50 for CYP450 isozymes inhibition could not be determined (IC50 was higher than the highest concentration tested). There was no time-dependent inhibition of CYP450 enzymes when incubated with inotuzumab ozogamicin or unconjugated calicheamicin
CYP450 Induction
Inotuzumab ozogamicin and unconjugated calicheamicin did not induce mRNA expression or enzymatic activities of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 in hepatocytes in vitro.
UGT Inhibition
Unconjugated calicheamicin inhibited the activities of UGT1A4, UGT1A6, UGT1A9, and UGT2B7 in vitro with IC50 values >10 µM. The IC50 of UGT1A1 in vitro inhibition was 0.61 µM, which is orders of magnitude higher than the expected CMAX at steady state (0.000034 µM).
Transporter Inhibition
Unconjugated calicheamicin exhibited no inhibition of P-gp or BCRP in vitro. Similarly, there was no inhibition of the renal uptake transporters OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2 in vitro. There was minimal inhibition of the hepatic uptake transporters OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 (30% and 16% inhibition) in vitro at 0.1 mM concentration, which is orders of magnitude higher than the expected CMAX.

EXCRETION	
Primary Excretion Pathway	
In preclinical species, feces, via biliary elimination, is the main route of excretion following IV doses of inotuzumab ozogamicin. Urinary excretion accounting for approximately 9% of the dose. No mass-balance studies were conducted in human subjects.
L370: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L371: ## Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L372: Yes, the clinical pharmacology data provide supportive evidence of effectiveness. The applicant submitted results of two key studies to support the proposed BLA (Table 7). The first study was a phase 1/2 dose escalation and expansion trial. The second study was a pivotal phase 3 study
L373: designed to evaluate safety and efficacy of inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to available treatment. Results of the dose selection and the pivotal studies are detailed below.
L374: **Table 7:**Clinical Trials supporting inotuzumab ozogamicin approval
L375: Trial (N)	Description	Study Design	Primary Endpoint(s)	PK Population (N)
B1931010 (N=72)	Phase 1 dose escalation/expansion and Phase 2	Single-arm, open-label, escalating doses, salvage \ge 2, Ph- and Ph+	CR/CRi	72
B1931022 (N=326)	Pivotal Phase 3 trial	Randomized (1:1), open-label, salvage 1 and 2, Ph- and Ph+	CR/CRi & OS	133
L376: The pivotal study B1931022 was an open-label, randomized, multi-center, global study of inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to control treatment (physician's choice) in adult subjects with relapsed or refractory CD22+ B-cell ALL in Salvage 1 or 2. The primary endpoints were CR/CRi and overall survival. The supportive study B1931010 was a single-arm, multi-center, open-label dose escalation and dose expansion trial in subjects with relapsed or refractory CD22+ ALL.
L377: ## Dosing Regimen Selection
L378: ## *Dosing Frequency*
L379: To support the proposed fractionated dosing, the applicant submitted a literature report comparing the fractionated (weekly dosing) vs. single monthly dosing regimens in patients with relapsed or refractory B-cell ALL (PMID: 23633004). The study compared a single dose 1.8 mg/m2 and a fractionated dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin administered on days 1 (0.8 mg/m2), 8 (0.5 mg/m2), and 15 (0.5 mg/m2) of a 21- to 28-day treatment cycle. The length of the first cycle depended on the recovery of blast counts as well as bone marrow status.
L380: The overall remission rate was 57% and 59% in the monthly and fractionated dosing regimens, respectively. The incidence of adverse events—drug-related fevers, elevated serum bilirubin, and elevated transaminases—were less common with the divided dosing regimen. Monthly dosing was associated with higher rates of liver function abnormalities, occasional VOD after allogeneic SCT, and transient febrile and hypotensive episodes (Table 8). The study determined that fractionated dosing was a safer alternative to the monthly dosing.
L381: **Table 8:** Frequency of Grade 3-4 adverse events following singly monthly and fractionated dosing
L382: Grade 3-4 Adverse Event	Monthly (Single) Dose N = 49	Fractionated Dosing n = 41
Drug-Related Fever, d 1-2	9	6
L383: Drug-Related Hypotension, d 1-2	1	0
Raised Bilirubin Concentration	2	0
Raised Aminotransferase Concentration	1	2
Raised Lipase or Amylase Concentration	1	0
Mucositis	1	0
VOD in Patients with Allogeneic Stem Cell Transplant	5/22	1/14
L384: ## *Dose Selection (Study B1931010)*
L385: The applicant implemented fractionated inotuzumab ozoagamicin dosing in study B1931010, which was a combined phase 1 dose escalation/ expansion and phase 2 study. In the dose escalation phase, study subjects were treated with escalating doses of 1.2 mg/m2, 1.6 mg/m2, or 1.8 mg/m2 to determine the safety, tolerability, preliminary efficacy, and recommended Phase 2 dose of single-agent inotuzumab ozogamicin (Table 9). The study was divided in the 3 parts: dose escalation, dose expansion, and phase 2.
L386: **Table 9:** Study B1931010 dose escalation and expansion scheme
L387: Study Phase	N	Schedule	Dose/Cycle (mg/m2)
D1	D8	D15
1: Dose Escalation	3	0.8	0.4	1.2
12	0.8	0.4	0.4	1.6
9	0.8	0.5	0.5	1.8
1: Expansion	13	0.8	0.5	0.5	1.8
2	0.8	0.5	0.5	1.8
L388: In the dose escalation part of the study, the rate of CR/CRI was 89% at the 1.8 mg/m2 dose level compared to 66.7% at the 1.2 mg/m2 dose. Additionally, the duration of remission was substantially longer and the median time to remission was shorter at 1.8 mg/m2 (Figure 3).
L389: Analysis of the safety data from the dose escalation part did not identify a correlation between dose and the rate of grade 3+ adverse events or the rate of treatment discontinuation. However, 4 cases of VOD occurred in patients receiving the 1.8 mg/m2 dose. Based on the safety and efficacy data, the 1.8 mg/m2 dose was selected for the dose expansion and Phase 2 parts of the study.
L390: Data from the dose expansion and phase 2 parts, however, suggest that the efficacy of 1.8 mg/m2 may not be substantially higher than that of 1.2 mg/m2 (Figure 3). The rate of CR/CRI, median time to MRD, and duration of remission in the expansion and phase 2 portions were similar to values achieved at the lower doses in the dose escalation portion. As such, the dose escalation part of the study did not provide complete information regarding the safety and efficacy properties of inotuzumab ozoagamicin at various dose levels.
L391: Figure 3: Efficacy results of the dose escalation (1.2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2, 1.6 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2, and 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 in the dose escalation, dose expansion (1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2), and phase 2 (1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2). (A) Rate of CR/CRi. (B)
L392: Median time to remission or MRD negativity. (C) Duration of remission.
L393: ## Body Surface Area Normalized Dosing
L394: Population PK analysis identified a statistically significant relationship between body surface area and inotuzumab ozogamicin clearance and volume of distribution (see section 13.4.2), thus supporting BSA based dosing. Clearance estimates increased by approximately 36% for BSA of 2.21 \text{ m}^2 (90th percentile) and decreased by 24% at BBSA of 1.56 \text{ m}^2 (10th percentile), relative to the typical value at 1.84 \text{ m}^2. Similarly, volume of distribution estimates increased by 28.8% for BBSA of 2.21 \text{ m}^2 and decreased by 21.6% at BBSA of 1.56 \text{ m}^2, relative to the typical value at 1.84 \text{ m}^2.
L395: ## Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L396: The data and information are insufficient to determine whether the proposed dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin is appropriate. As detailed above, the 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 dose was selected for the pivotal trial evaluation based efficacy data from the phase 1/2 trial. The pivotal trial B1931022 was an open-label, randomized, multi-center, global study of inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to control treatment (physician's choice) in adult subjects with relapsed or refractory CD22+ B-cell ALL in Salvage 1 or 2. The primary endpoints were CR/CRi and overall survival. The CR/CRi rate was 73.2% (95% CI: 65.7-79.8%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 30.9% (95% CI: 23.9-38.6%) in the control arm. The stratified hazard ratio for overall survival was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.578 - 1.026) and the unstratified hazard ratio was 0.748 (95% CI: 0.563 - 0.993).
L397: ## Exposure-efficacy relationship
L398: Exposure-response analyses for safety and efficacy were conducted using data collected from the Phase 3 trial at a dose of 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (n = 164) and the supportive phase 1/2 trial at doses of 1.2 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (n=2), 1.6 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (n=12), and 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (n =57). Based the data are primarily obtained from a single dose level trial, the exposure-response information presented herein should be interpreted with caution. Detailed exposure-response data sources and analyses are described in section 13.4.1).
L399: Exposure-response analysis for efficacy identified a positive correlation between the cumulative AUC in the first cycle (cAUCP1) and average concentration (C_{AVG}), defined as the cumulative AUC during treatment normalized by the time duration, and the probability of achieving CR/CRi (Figure 4 and Figure 5).
L400: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L401: **Figure 4:** Predicted probability of CR/CRI response with Inotuzumab Ozogamicin treatment for C_{AVG} when stratified by prior salvage therapy or percent CD22-positivity. Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-205 applicant's exposure-response analysis.
L402: **Figure 5:** Predicted probability of CR/CRI response with Inotuzumab Ozogamicin treatment for cAUCP1 when stratified by prior salvage therapy or percent CD22-positivity. Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-205 applicant's exposure-response analysis.
L403: The probability of achieving CR/CRi was also influenced by the number of prior salvage therapies (1 vs &gt;1) and percentage of CD22-positivity. The probability of achieving CR/CRi was higher in patients with only one prior salvage therapy and &gt;90% CD22+ leukemic blasts at baseline. Based on the goodness-of-fit criteria and the evaluation of model diagnostics, C_{AVG} was the better predictor of probability of CR/CRi compared to cAUCP1.
L404: ## Exposure-Safety Relationship
L405: The rates of grade 3+ adverse events and the most common adverse events (thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, anemia, leukopenia, and febrile neutropenia) were either similar or more favorable in the inotuzumab arm compared to the control arm. However, the rate of VOD was higher in the inotuzumab arm (13.4%) compared to the control arm (0.1%). In the subset of patients who proceeded to HSCT, the rate of VOD was substantially higher in the inotuzumab arm (22.1%) compared to the control arm (3.1%).
L406: Exposure-response analysis for safety did not identify a relationship between inotuzumab exposure and grade 3+ ALT, grade 3+ AST, grade 3+ bilirubin, or hepatic events (defined by a pre-specified cluster term). However, there was a statistically significant relationship between the AUCP1 and HEAB-assessed VOD/ sinusoidal obstruction syndrome (SOS) (Figure 6). Post-treatment HSCT therapy was also found to be a statistically significant predictor of VOD. For more details refer to the Appendices.
L407: **Figure 6:** Predicted probability of HEAB-assessed VOD/SOS as a function of cumulative AUC after the first cycle of treatment (cAUCP1). Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-205 applicant's exposure-response analysis.
L408: Collectively, data from the pivotal efficacy study support the clinical benefit of inotuzumab ozogamicin in B-cell precursor ALL at the proposed dose of 1.8 \text{ mg/m}^2. However, due to the very high rate of VOD and the corresponding post-HSCT non-relapse mortality (see Clinical Review), the dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin needs to be further optimized. The applicant will be asked to conduct a post-marketing dose finding study. Prior clinical experience with the
L409: similar ADC gemtuzumab ozogamicin—a CD33 mAb linked to calicheamicin—in patients with AML demonstrated that the rates of VOD increased from 1% at the time of approval to 10% at the time of withdrawal (15% in patients who progressed to SCT). Compiled data from the FDA mandated Prospective Observational Registry, FDA Adverse Events Reporting Systems, The Research on Adverse Drug Events and Reports (RADAR), and Southern Network on Adverse Reactions (SONAR) databases demonstrated that the rate of VOD decreased from 15% at the approved 9 \text{ mg/m}^2 dose to 3% at doses \le 6 \text{ mg/m}^2 (PMID:27030962). (b) (4)
L410: previously labeled dose of gemtuzumab ozogamicin (9 \text{ mg/m}^2) and the proposed dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin (1.8 \text{ mg/m}^2). Therefore, the gemtuzumab ozogamicin experience suggests a lower dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin may lead to lower rates of VOD, which will be explored in the agency proposed PMR study.
L411: ## Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L412: No, an alternative dosing regimen and management strategy is not required based on age, sex and race. Refer to section “13. Error! Reference source not found. Population PK Analysis” for a detailed discussion.
L413: APPEARS THIS WAY ON
ORIGINAL
L414: ## Renal Impairment
L415: No dose adjustment is required for patients with renal impairment. The effect of renal function on inotuzumab ozogamicin clearance was evaluated using baseline creatinine clearance (BCCL) in patients with normal renal function (n=377), mild (n= 234), moderate (n=121), and severe impairment (n=4). Renal impairment did not influence CL of inotuzumab ozogamicin (Figure 7). Additionally, calicheamicin exhibited minimal clearance in the urine in preclinical species and is not likely to be excreted in the urine based on *in vitro* studies.
L416: The rate of thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, VOD, and ALT adverse events was similar among patients with normal, mild, or moderate renal impairment in the phase 3 trial (Figure 8). Similarly, the rate of discontinuation due to adverse events was not correlated with normal (13%), mild (18%), and moderate (0%) renal impairment.
L417: **Figure 7:** Renal impairment stage does not impact inotuzumab ozogamicin clearance. Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-202 applicant's population PK analysis.
L418: **Figure 8:** Frequency of grade 3+ thrombocytopenia (A), neutropenia (B), VOD (C), or elevated ALT (D) in patients with normal, mild, or moderate renal impairment
L419: ## Hepatic Impairment
L420: No dose adjustment is required for patients with hepatic impairment. The impact of hepatic impairment on the clearance of inotuzumab ozogamicin was evaluated using the hepatic function defined by the NCI ODWG (normal [A], mild [B1], mild [B2], moderate [C], and severe [D]) as categorical covariate and baseline laboratory values of BALT, BAST, and BBIL as continuous covariates in the population PK model (section 13.4.2). Hepatic impairment stage classifications and distributions of patients included in the population PK analysis are summarized in Table 10.
L421: Table 10: Classification and distribution of patients among with hepatic impairment
L422: N	NCI ODWG	BIL	AST
611	Normal (A)	\le ULN	\le ULN

L423: Hepatic impairment was not a significant covariate on the CL of inotuzumab ozogamicin; CL was similar in patients with different hepatic impairment stages (Figure 9).
L424: Figure 9: Hepatic impairment does not impact inotuzumab ozogamicin clearance. Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-202 applicant's population PK analysis.
L425: The rate of thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, VOD, and ALT adverse events was not correlated with the hepatic impairment status (Figure 10). Additionally, the rate of treatment discontinuation due to adverse events was similar between patients with normal (13/121) and mild hepatic impairment (7/41) in the phase 3 trial. Only a single patient had severe hepatic impairment, which precluded safety analysis in this population.
L426: Due to the hepatotoxicity observed with inotuzumab ozogamicin, liver function tests are closely monitored in patients, especially patients with liver disease, as per the product label. Dose interruption is recommended for patients with total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 \times ULN and AST/ALT &gt; 2.5 \times ULN prior to each dose unless due to Gilbert's syndrome or hemolysis. The recommendation is based on the adverse event profile of inotuzumab ozogamicin and because
L427: there are no safety data for patients with total bilirubin &gt;1.5 \times \text{ULN} and AST/ALT &gt; 2.5 \times \text{ULN}, which was one of the exclusion criteria in the pivotal efficacy trial.
L428: **Figure 10:** Frequency of grade 3+ thrombocytopenia (A), neutropenia (B), VOD (C), or elevated ALT (D) in patients with normal, mild, or moderate hepatic impairment.
L429: The proposed lack of dose adjustment for patients with hepatic impairment is acceptable given the close monitoring of liver tests in all patients and proposed dose interruptions for patients with elevated AST/ALT and bilirubin. Additionally, a dedicated trial of inotuzumab, with such a hepatotoxic profile, in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment is unethical.
L430: ## Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L431: There are no clinically relevant drug-drug interactions anticipated based on the *in vitro* screening.
L432: N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin is metabolized via non-enzymatic reduction, with no CYP450 isozymes or UGT enzymes involvement. Additionally, *in vitro* studies demonstrated that inotuzumab ozogamicin and unconjugated N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin inhibit CYP450 isozymes and UGT enzymes at concentrations that are orders of magnitude higher than the anticipated clinical C_{\text{MAX}}. Similarly, unconjugated calicheamicin demonstrated low potential to inhibit P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2 *in vitro*.
L433: The population PK modeling (see section 13.4.2) did not identify statistically significant covariates affecting inotuzumab ozogamicin clearance due to interaction with other drugs. The investigated drugs are summarized in **Table 11**. According to the population PK model, treatment with rituximab decreased the clearance of inotuzumab ozogamicin in patients with
L434: NHL by 16% (95% CI: 5.47%-26.5%). Conclusions from the population PK analyses should be interpreted with caution and are only considered supportive. The administration of concomitant medications relative to that of inotuzumab was not included in the model. Concomitant medications were included as a dichotomous variable.

L454: ## 7.1.2. Review Strategy
L455: The FDA clinical and statistical review consisted of one primary clinical reviewer and one primary statistical reviewer.
L456: The key materials used for the review of efficacy and safety include the following:
L457: - BLA 761040
- Relevant published literature
- Relevant information in the public domain
L458: The review of efficacy was primarily based on analysis of Study 1022 and supported as follows:
L459: - Efficacy data from Study 1010 was used to support the analysis of dose selection
- Summarized efficacy data from study 1010
- Efficacy data from Study 1006 and Study 1008 were used to include lack of efficacy in patients with NHL.
L460: The primary review of safety was based on the pivotal trial (Study 1022) and the supportive efficacy and safety study (1010). Due to differences in dosing regimens, different diseases and patient populations, only the NHL studies that used single-agent inotuzumab ozogamicin were reviewed for supportive safety analysis. These studies include B1931002, B1931007 and B1931016. Refer to table 7 for further details.
L461: - Individual study data from Study 1022
- Review and assessment of Applicant analyses of Study 1022 efficacy and safety in the Clinical Study Report
- Review of datasets submitted as SAS transport files
- Review of minutes of key meetings conducted during inotuzumab ozogamicin clinical development
- Pooled safety data from Study 1010 and Study 1022 to support the analysis of the proposed patient population and proposed dose.
- Pooled safety data from single agent inotuzumab ozogamicin studies to look for potential safety signals.
L462: All major efficacy and safety analyses were reproduced or audited. Summaries of data and statistical analyses by the reviewer were performed using SAS version 9.4 and JMP 12.0(SAS institute, Cary, NC). MedDRA Adverse Events Diagnostic 1.3(MAED)(FDA, Silver Spring, MD) was used to look for safety signals.

L470: ## 7.2. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L471: ## Study B1931022: An Open-Label, Randomized Phase 3 Study of Inotuzumab Ozogamicin Compared to a Defined Investigator's Choice in Adult Patients with Relapsed or Refractory CD22 Positive Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL)
L472: ## Trial Design
L473: B1931022 (also referred to as Study 1022) was a multicenter, global, open-label, 2-arm randomized (1:1) phase 3 study, designed to compare the efficacy of inotuzumab ozogamicin (Arm A, investigational arm) versus defined Investigator's choice of chemotherapy (Arm B, control arm), in adult patients with relapsed or refractory CD22-positive B-cell ALL due to receive either Salvage 1 or 2 treatment. Defined Investigator's choice of chemotherapy (control arm therapy) was included either fludarabine + cytarabine + granulocyte-colony stimulating factor (G-CSF) (FLAG), mitoxantrone + cytarabine (MXN/Ara-C), or high-dose cytarabine (HIDAC).
L474: Patients in Arm A were treated with inotuzumab ozogamicin at a starting dose of 1.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (according to BSA) per cycle with a divided-dose regimen using 3 weekly administrations. Patients received 0.8 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2/cycle on Week 1 (Day1), following by 0.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 on Week 2 (Day8) and Week 3 (Day 15) of a 21-day cycle administered as an intravenous (IV) infusion over 60 minutes. For patients who achieved a CR or CRI, or to allow recovery from toxicity, the length of Cycle 1 could be extended up to 28 days (i.e., 1 week treatment-free interval starting on Day 21). For patients who achieved CR or CRI, the Week 1 dose was reduced to 0.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2 (for a total dose of 1.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2/cycle) for Cycles 2 through 6 (maximum number of cycles permitted). For Cycle 2 through 6, the cycle length was 28 days for all patients (regardless of remission status).
L475: *Reviewer Comment: Control Group (Comparator Group):
In the salvage setting of ALL, a variety of regimens are used ranging from single agents to*
L476: *combination therapies. Patients with Ph+ ALL are treated with combinations including tyrosine kinase inhibitors. In this protocol, it is expected that most patients will have been treated with intensive combination chemotherapy and that physicians will select therapies based on patient's condition, prior therapies and standard practice. The comparator arm includes a defined list of 3 induction chemotherapy regimens commonly used in relapsed and refractory ALL.*
L477: ## Diagnostic Criteria
L478: The patient population consists of patients with relapsed or refractory CD22-positive ALL (&gt;5% marrow blasts) assessed by morphology due to receive either salvage 1 or salvage 2 and for which either arm of the randomized study therapy offers a reasonable option. Patients with Ph+ ALL must have failed at least 1 second or third generation tyrosine kinase inhibitor and standard multi-agent chemotherapy.
L479: ## *Reviewer Comment:*
L480: *CD22 is expressed in over 90% of lymphoblasts in patients with ALL. Standard diagnostic evaluation of patients with relapsed or refractory ALL includes flow cytometry or immunohistochemistry both of which include analysis of CD22. Given the routine testing of CD markers in the diagnostic evaluation of patients with ALL and the high prevalence of CD22 positivity in lymphoblasts this reviewer recommends no restriction on CD22+ testing.*
L481: Patients were stratified at randomization based on the following baseline characteristics:
L482: - Duration of first remission (&lt;12 months or ≥12 months);
- Salvage treatment that the patient was to receive on study (Salvage 1 or 2);Salvage 1 was defined as therapy given in response to morphological relapse after initial treatment or resistant disease (no CR or CRi) after initial treatment;
- Salvage 2 was defined as therapy given in response to morphological relapse after Salvage 1 therapy or resistant disease (no CR or CRi) after Salvage 1;
- Patient age at randomization (&lt;55 or ≥55 years).
L483: Patients were allowed to receive study treatment for up to 6 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin and for up to 4 cycles of the Investigator's choice of FLAG or MXN/Ara-C, and up to 2 cycles of HIDAC; these regimens are all considered to be standard of care. Study drug(s) could be discontinued at any time due to initiation of new anti-cancer therapy (ie, conditioning for HSCT), disease progression, patient refusal, or unacceptable toxicity, whichever occurred first. Patients who achieved a response to treatment and who had a suitable donor could proceed to allogeneic HSCT at the discretion of the Investigator and were followed for disease progression and survival. Patients whose treatment was stopped early due to toxicity or other reasons were followed for safety, disease progression and survival. Use of anti-cancer treatments, other than those allowed by the protocol, were prohibited during the active treatment phase. After discontinuation of study treatment, subsequent anti-cancer therapy was per Investigator's discretion.
L484: Following discontinuation of study treatment, patients were followed for:
L485: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L486: - Disease progression for up 2 years from randomization, unless the patient progressed during treatment;
- Potential onset of VOD/SOS, irrespective of grade, causality or treatment arm for up to 2 years from randomization;
- Survival for up to 5 years or 2 year from randomization of the last patient, whichever occurred first.
L487: The study was planned to be conducted in approximately 190 sites over a period of approximately 4 years.
L488: ## **Primary Efficacy Endpoint**
L489: The study has two primary endpoints which include hematologic remission (CR+CRi) assessed by the Endpoint Adjudication Committee (EAC) and OS.
L490: ## **Hematologic remission (CR/CRi) assessed by EAC**
L491: CR was defined as disappearance of leukemia as indicated by &lt;5% marrow blasts and the absence of peripheral blood leukemic blasts, with recovery of hematopoiesis defined by absolute neutrophil count (ANC) \ge 1000/\mu\text{L}, platelets \ge 100,000/\mu\text{L}, and resolution of any extramedullary disease.
L492: CRi was defined as CR except with absolute neutrophil count (ANC) &lt; 1000/\mu\text{L} and/or platelets &lt; 100,000/\mu\text{L}.
L493: In addition to assessment by the EAC, remission was also assessed by investigators.
L494: ## **Overall survival**
L495: OS was defined as the time from randomization to death due to any cause. For patients last known to be alive, OS was censored at date of last contact.
L496: ## **Reviewer's Comments**
L497: *As stated in the protocol CR/CRi was referred to as the primary endpoint and OS was referred to as a key secondary endpoint. In SAP v3.0, it was clarified that OS was 1 of the 2 primary endpoints.*
L498: ## **Secondary Efficacy Endpoints**
L499: Secondary endpoints include the following
L500: - Progression-free survival (PFS): defined as time from date of randomization to earliest date of the following events:Death
- Progressive diseaseObjective progression
- Relapse from CR/CRi
- Treatment discontinuation due to global deterioration of health status
- Starting new induction therapy or post-therapy SCT without achieving CR/CRi.
L501: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L502: Observations for subjects without a PFS event at time of analysis were planned to be censored at the last valid disease assessment. In addition, observation for subjects with documentation of an event after an unacceptably long interval (&gt;28 weeks if there was post-baseline disease assessment, or &gt;12 weeks if there was no post-baseline assessment) since the previous disease assessment would be censored at the time of the previous assessment (date of randomization if no post-baseline assessment). Post-study treatment follow-up disease assessments would be included. In addition duration of remission and minimal residual disease status were also captured.
L503: - Duration of remission (DoR)
DoR is defined as time from date of first response in responders (CR/CRI by investigator assessment) to date of PFS event (see definition of PFS endpoint above). Responders without PFS event were censored at the last valid disease assessment including follow-up disease assessment. Note: DoR is defined as duration of response in the protocol, but is changed to duration of remission in this SAP.
- Minimal residual disease (MRD) levels (positive, negative) and cytogenetics in patients achieving a CR/CRI (investigators assessed and centrally reviewed).
L504: ## Key Inclusion Criteria (summarized)
L505: - Relapsed or refractory CD22-positive ALL(\ge 5\% marrow blasts, assessed by morphology) due to receive either salvage 1 or salvage 2 therapy and for which either arm of randomized study therapy offers a reasonable option.
- Ph+ ALL patients must have failed treatment with at least 1 second or third generation tyrosine kinase inhibitor and standard multi-agent induction chemotherapy.
- Patients in salvage 1 with late relapsed should be deemed poor candidates for re-induction with initial therapy.
- Patients with lymphoblastic lymphoma and bone marrow involvement \ge 5\% lymphoblasts by morphological assessment.
- Age \ge 18 years
- Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group(ECOG) PS of 0-2.
- Adequate liver function including total serum bilirubin \le 1.5 \times upper limit of normal (ULN) unless the patient has Gilbert syndrome and AST and ALT \le 2.5 \times ULN. If organ function abnormalities are considered due to tumor, total serum bilirubin must be \le 2 \times ULN and AST/ALT \le 2.5 \times ULN.
- Serum creatinine \le 1.5 \times ULN or any serum creatinine level associated with a measured or calculated creatinine clearance of \ge 40\text{ml/min}.
- Male or female patients of childbearing potential and at risk for pregnancy must agree to use a highly effective method of contraception throughout the study and for a minimum of 90 days after the last dose of assigned treatment.
L506: ## Key Exclusion Criteria
L507: - Isolated extramedullary relapse
L508: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L509: - Burkitt's or mixed phenotype acute leukemia based on the WHO 2008 criteria.
- Active central nervous system (CNS) leukemia, use of CNS-directed local treatment for active disease within 28 days prior, symptomatic CNS leukemia within 28 days. Prophylactic intrathecal medication is not a reason for exclusion.
- Prior chemotherapy within 2 weeks before randomization with the following exceptions:To reduce circulating lymphoblast count or palliation.
- For ALL maintenance: mercaptopurine, methotrexate, vincristine, thioguanine and/or tyrosine kinase inhibitors.
- Patients must have recovered from acute non-hematological toxicity (to \leGrade 1) of all previous therapy prior to enrollment.
- Prior monoclonal antibodies within 6 weeks of randomization with the exception of rituximab which must be discontinued at least 2 weeks prior to randomization.
- Prior allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HSCT) or other anti-CD22 immunotherapy \le 4 months before randomization.
- Known systemic vasculitides, primary or secondary immunodeficiency.
- Current or chronic hepatitis B or C infection or known seropositivity for human immunodeficiency virus (HIV).
- Cardiac function as measured by left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) that is less than 45% or the presence of New York Heart Association stage III or IV congestive heart failure.
- QTcF &gt; 470msec (based on average of 3 consecutive ECGs).
- Myocardial infarction within \le 6 months before randomization.
- History of clinically significant ventricular arrhythmia or unexplained syncope.
- Uncontrolled electrolyte disorders that can compound the effects of a QTc prolonging drug.
- History of chronic liver disease or history of hepatic VOD.
- Pregnant females, breastfeeding females, males and females of childbearing potential not using highly effective contraceptive techniques or not agreeing to continue highly effective contraception for a minimum of 90 days after the last dose of investigational product.
L510: ## Dose Modification and Delays
L511: Patients in inotuzumab ozogamicin and control arm must meet the following criteria prior to the start of each cycle or else all study therapy will be delayed:
L512: - No evidence of progressive disease (PD)
- Decrease in blast percentage or stable disease
- Recovery to grade 1 or baseline non-hematologic test article-related toxicity
- Serum bilirubin \le 1.5 x ULN (or if elevated due to tumor, bilirubin \le 2 x ULN and AST, ALT \le 2.5 x ULN irrespective of the causality (prior to each dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin)).
- Serum creatinine \le 2 x ULN or estimated creatinine clearance \ge 40 mL/min as calculated using the method standard for the institution (prior to each dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin).
L513: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L514: - For patients with pre-treatment ANC \ge 1 \times 10^9/L: ANC \ge 1 \times 10^9/L
- For patients with pre-treatment platelets \ge 50 \times 10^9/L; platelets \ge 50 \times 10^9/L and platelets must recover at least to baseline values obtained for the prior cycle, or ANC &gt; 1 \times 10^9/L and platelets and &gt; 50 \times 10^9/L or the most recent bone marrow must demonstrate stable or improved disease and the ANC and platelets are believed to be low due to disease.
- QTcF \le 470 \text{ msec} (average 3 ECGs)
L515: Inotuzumab ozogamicin dosing should be permanently discontinued for any patients with possible, probable or confirmed VOD or other severe liver toxicity. For inotuzumab ozogamicin, dose delays \ge 7 days, will result in omission of the next dose within the cycle; the patient remains eligible to receive the subsequent planned dose assuming all dosing criteria are met. If a dose within a cycle is delayed, the next dose will be delayed. A minimum of 6 days should be maintained between doses. If the beginning of the next cycle is delayed by more than 28 days of the current cycle, then the day when treatment is restarted will be considered Day 1 of the next cycle. If the beginning of the next cycle is delayed by more than 28 days due to test article-related toxicity/ies study treatment will be permanently discontinued unless otherwise agreed by sponsor and investigator.
L516: ## Dose Reductions
L517: In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, patients experiencing a treatment interruption dose to test article-related toxicity \ge 14 days will resume dosing with a single 25% reduction in inotuzumab ozogamicin for the subsequent cycle (0.5 \text{ mg/m}^2 to 0.375 \text{ mg/m}^2) once adequate recovery is achieved. If further dose modification is indicated after a 25% dose reduction, the number of dose within the cycle should be reduced to two for subsequent cycles. Dose reductions of inotuzumab ozogamicin by 25% are recommended for patients with CRI, whose platelet counts have not recovered to those values obtained prior to the start of previous cycle.
L518: Dose reductions or omissions of drug for Arm B (investigator choice arm) should be based on institutional guidelines or standard of care.
L519: ## Concomitant Medications
L520: The use of hydroxyurea is permitted for temporary control of WBC elevations in patients with aggressive disease both prior and during the first 5 days of study treatment. Reduction of peripheral blast counts to at least 10,000/\mu\text{L} is required for randomization and may improve the initial PK profile of inotuzumab ozogamicin. Concurrent therapy for CNS prophylaxis treatment is strongly encouraged. Growth factors such as granulocyte-colony stimulating factor (G-CSF), including pegfilgastrim and GM-CSF may be used as supportive care with each cycle if clinically indicated after the last dose of study drug or chemotherapy in accordance with local guidelines and medical practice.
L521: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
{BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L522: Prohibited concomitant medications: craniospinal radiation (CSXRT) is prohibited during study treatment. Medications known to predispose to Torsade's de Pointes are prohibited throughout the treatment period of study.
L523: ## Administrative Structure
L524: The study also used an external Hepatic Events Adjudication Board which reviewed safety data with respect to hepatic events. The HEAB was a blinded external expert advisory group composed by a chair and 2 other board members (all board certified hepatologists with expertise in VOD/SOS and drug induced liver injury (DILI) established to adjudicate hepatobiliary events occurring in Study 1022 as detailed in the HEAB Charter.
L525: ## Definition of VOD on protocol
L526: VOD was defined in Study 1022 as the occurrence of 2 out of the following clinical criteria: total serum bilirubin level &gt;2.0\text{mg/dL}, an increase in liver size from baseline or development of right upper quadrant pain of live origin, sudden weight gain &gt;2.5\% due to fluid accumulation in the weeks following infusion of study drug or chemotherapy, or HSCT conditioning/preparative regimen or development of ascites not present at baseline following such exposures, and the absence of other explanations for these signs and symptoms. In addition, VOD was also defined as development of bilirubin elevation, weight gain or hepatomegaly plus histologic abnormalities on liver biopsy demonstrating hepatocyte necrosis in zone 3 of the liver acinus, sinusoidal fibrosis and centrilobular hemorrhage with or without fibrosis of the terminal hepatic venules.
L527: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L528: ## Sample Size and Power
L529: The sample size was calculated to assess the difference between the 2 treatment arms in hematologic remission (CR/CRi) and OS separately by splitting 1-sided alpha of 0.025 evenly between the 2 primary endpoints.
L530: Based on a review of the literature, the expected CR/CRi rate in the control arm (first and second salvage setting) was approximately 37% assuming that up to one third of patients may receive Salvage 2. Remission rates in the Salvage 1 setting have typically been reported between 30% and 50% with remission rates in the Salvage 2 setting being typically much lower at around 16% to 18%. With 218 patients, the study had at least 88.5% power to detect a difference of response probabilities between 61% in inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus 37% in the control arm for 1-sided \alpha=0.0125 for the analysis based on the intent to treat population (ITT). In order to maintain design properties, the original sample size was increased in protocol Amendment 3 (dated 28 March 2014) due to a higher than expected number of early discontinuations after randomization in the control arm prior to dosing, and also due to a higher number of patients lost to survival follow-up for those randomized to the control arm.
L531: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L532: The study was also designed to detect a difference in OS (hazard ratio [HR] 0.67 corresponding to 50% improvement in OS, i.e., this translated to an improvement in the median survival from 4.30 months on defined Investigator's choice of chemotherapy to 6.45 months on inotuzumab ozogamicin) with 80% power for 1-sided \alpha=0.0125. Assuming accrual of 2 patients per month for the first 6 months and 9.33 patients per month thereafter, 20% dropout within 15 days and 25% dropout total in the defined Investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm, and 5% dropout total in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, a total sample size of 325 patients was required. It was planned that accrual would continue to a target sample size for OS of 325 patients unless the External Data Monitoring Committee eDMC stopped accrual earlier and that the final analysis for OS would be performed after 248 OS events, if accrual of 325 patients was achieved.
L533: For the OS endpoint, the study used a group sequential design with 2 interim analyses with the opportunity to stop the study for futility at the first interim analysis. At the second interim analysis, the study could have been stopped for futility or efficacy. If the second interim analysis occurred at exactly 149 events, the study could be stopped for futility if the p-value (p) was greater than 0.17 or for efficacy if p &lt; 0.003.
L534: ## Analysis Population
L535: Intent-to Treat (ITT) Population: The ITT population included all subjects who are randomized into the study and it was primary population for efficacy evaluation.
L536: Modified Intent-to-Treat (mITT) Population: included all randomized patients who started study treatment.
L537: Per Protocol Population (PP): The PP included patients who met all of the following criteria: randomized and received at least 1 dose of study drug; no major protocol violations and had an adequate baseline disease assessment.
L538: Safety Population: included all randomized subjects who receive at least 1 dose of treatment (either inotuzumab ozogamicin or investigator's choice). This patient population excluded only subjects who never received any treatment or were not randomized.
L539: ## Missing Data
L540: If any pattern of missing data was identified, the analysis plan stated that additional efforts such as sensitivity analysis will be made to ensure the analysis is not unduly affected by the missing data. For time to event variables, missing data are handled by censoring observations. All subjects would be followed for PFS until a PFS event has occurred or until the data cutoff. Sensitivity analyses may be performed to assess the robustness of the result of the primary analysis of PFS. Patients without post-baseline tumor assessment, including patients who did not receive any treatment, are counted as non-responders (did not achieve CR or CRi) for the
L541: primary analysis. Sensitivity analyses for CR/CRi will be done to address missing response information in patients who discontinue the study prior to treatment
L542: ## **Statistical Methodologies**
L543: The 2 primary endpoints (CR/CRi and OS) were tested separately by splitting alpha evenly between them.
L544: Final analysis of hematologic remission (CR/CRi), DoR, and MRD were to be performed after the first 218 randomized patients had been followed for at least 3 months after randomization.
L545: The primary endpoint of OS was planned to be analyzed at 2 interim analyses and final analysis. The 2 planned interim analyses were conducted when approximately 25% and at least 60% of the required OS events were reached (for futility [first interim analyses], and efficacy and futility [second interim analyses]). The final analysis for OS is planned to occur after approximately 248 (100%) of OS events have been reported. the interim OS analysis results are not included in this CSR.
L546: ## **Primary Analysis of CR/CRi**
L547: The primary analysis of CR/CRi was based on the ITT218 Population which included the first 218 randomized patients. Patients were analyzed in the randomized treatment arms regardless of the actual treatment given. Patients who did not achieve CR/CRi, including patients who did not receive study treatment, were considered as non-responders.
L548: Assuming delta (\Delta) was the true difference in the probability of CR/CRi between the 2 treatment arms (inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus control arm), the hypotheses were H0: \Delta \le 0 versus Ha: \Delta &gt; 0. CR/CRi rates were compared between the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and the control arm using the Chi-square test or Fisher's exact test (if any cell size was under 5) at 1-sided \alpha=0.0125 significance level. For each treatment arm, the CR/CRi rate along with the 95% CI around the rate was computed. The estimate of \Delta and its 97.5% CI was computed. CIs of CR/CRi rates for the 2 treatment arms were computed using the F-distribution method. CIs of \Delta was computed using normal approximation.
L549: ## **Sensitivity analysis for CR/CRi**
L550: Sensitivity analyses were planned to investigate the robustness of the primary analysis. The sensitivity analyses described below were performed based on CR/CRi per EAC. In addition, investigator's assessment performed based on CR/CRi was also analyzed. Sensitivity analyses were based on the mITT218 and the PP218 population.
L551: Analysis assuming untreated patients as responders:
L552: An ITT218 sensitivity analysis was conducted assuming all untreated patients were successes (achieved CR or CRi). This approach produced an overestimate of the probability of remission with the most extreme assumption, and would lead to bias favoring the treatment arm with more untreated patients and against the arm with fewer or no untreated patients. Assuming
L553: the probability of not receiving treatment on the control arm was 0.2 and that dropout and remission were independent, the null remission probability was 0.5 on the control arm instead of 0.37 and power was reduced from 88.5% to &lt;30%
L554: Logistic Regression Multiple Imputation (Missing at Random):
This sensitivity analysis was based on the first 218 randomized patients (ITT218), in which responses were imputed for untreated patients. Patients who started treatment and did not have a response assessment were assumed non-responders. This analysis was based on the assumption of missing at random (MAR), i.e., given observed data, the missing mechanism for response does not depend on the unobserved data, and the missing value mechanism for response can be expressed solely in terms of observations. Logistic regression was used to model the association between remission and observed baseline factors.
L555: This was a less biased approach compared to assigning either all success or all failure to patients who discontinued from the study after randomization but never receiving study treatment.
L556: Imputation was performed according to treatment arm. Therefore, only the control arm had imputed responses since all patients randomized to the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm received randomized treatment. The method was appropriate under the MAR assumption (but not missing completely at random [MCAR] because imputation was not needed if MCAR).
L557: A logistic regression model was fitted using treated patients.
L558: The probability of remission in the control arm was estimated by the mean of the estimates from the imputed complete datasets. The CI was calculated based on the estimate and the binomial distribution. Estimates of probability of remission between the inotuzumab ozogamicin and control arms were compared using a Chi-square test. For each imputed complete dataset, the Chi-square statistic was calculated based on the imputed responses (control arm) and observed data from the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. These Chi-square statistics were combined to calculate the p-value for comparing the treatment arms.
L559: ## **Primary Analysis of OS**
L560: The primary analysis of OS was based on the ITT population. Patients who did not have OS event were censored at the date they were last known to be alive.
L561: The null hypothesis for the primary efficacy comparison is that the distribution of OS between the 2 treatment groups is the same. The corresponding alternative hypothesis is that the distribution of OS between the 2 treatment groups is different and in favor of the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm.
L562: The OS of patients randomized to the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was compared with that of the control arm using the stratified log-rank test at a 1-sided 0.0125 significance level. The
L563: hazard ratio and corresponding 97.5% 2-sided CI using stratified Cox proportional hazard regression (same stratification factors as for randomization) is presented. The median OS was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method and is reported with 2-sided 95% CIs for each arm using the method described by Bookmeyer and Crowley. In addition, KM estimate of the survival functions was computed and plotted for each treatment arm.
L564: ## Sensitivity Analysis of OS
L565: Sensitivity analyses of OS were to include the following:
L566: - mITT analysis, excluding patients who did not receive study treatment;
- PP analysis based on the PP population;
- Censored at HSCT date: Sensitivity analyses were conducted by censoring OS at the time when a patient received first post-treatment HSCT
- Censored at Subsequent Therapies: Sensitivity analyses were conducted by censoring OS at the time when a patient received a specific or all subsequent therapies (e.g., censored for blinatumomab, censored for inotuzumab ozogamicin for control arm, or censored for follow-up induction therapies)
L567: ## FDA's Exploratory Post-Hoc Analysis of OS
L568: ## Competing Risk Analyses
L569: The usual technique for time-to-event analysis applied in the presence of competing risks may give biased or uninterpretable results. The estimation of the probability of the event using the cumulative incidence function is needed (Gray, The Annals of Statistics, 1988). Analyses for OS modeling either cause-specific hazard or subdistribution hazard of death due to relapse or progression were conducted for the ITT population, the patients in the ITT with follow-up HSCT, and for the patients in the ITT who achieved CR/CRI per Investigator's assessment, respectively. In these analyses, death due to relapsed or progression was the primary event of interest, while death due to cause other than relapse or progression was considered to be competing risk.
L570: ## Time dependent covariate Cox model
L571: One of the strengths of the Cox model is its ability to encompass covariates that changes over time. The practical reason that time-dependent covariates work is based on the underlying way in which the Cox model works: at each event time the program compares the current covariate values of the subject who had the event to the current values of all others who were at risk at that time. The model has a theoretical foundation in martingale theory.
L572: ## Analysis of Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
L573: ## Minimal Residual Disease (MRD)
L574: MRD-negativity was assessed in patients in the ITT Population who achieved CR/CRI per EAC assessment by the central laboratory(flow cytometry), as well as in all patients in the ITT Population. The MRD-negativity rate was compared between the 2 treatment arms. Subgroup analysis of MRD by type of hematologic remission (CR and CRI) was also conducted, respectively.
L575: ## Duration of Remission (DoR) and Duration of Complete Remission (DoCR)
L576: All the comparisons for DoR and DoCR specified in this section were conducted between the 2 arms using the 1-sided stratified log-rank test respectively. The HR and corresponding 95% 2-sided CI using stratified Cox PH regression were presented. The median DoRs were estimated using the KM method and were reported with 2-sided 95% CIs for each arm using the method described by Brookmeyer and Crowley. In addition, KM estimates of the survival function were computed and plotted for each treatment arm
L577: DoR and DoCR were analyzed in patients in the ITT218 Population who achieved CR/CRi per Investigator's assessment. The DoR and DoCR were compared between the 2 arms using the 1-sided stratified log-rank test respectively. The HR and corresponding 95% 2-sided CI using stratified Cox proportional hazard regression (same stratification factors as for randomization) were presented. The median DoRs were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method and were reported with 2-sided 95% CIs for each arm using the method described by Brookmeyer and Crowley (1982). In addition, Kaplan-Meier estimate of the survival functions were computed and plotted for each treatment arm.
L578: ## *Reviewer's Comments:*
L579: *Comparison of Duration of response are not valid, since having a response is a treatment related outcome, formal statistical comparisons of duration of response will not be interpretable.*
L580: ## Progression-Free Survival (PFS)
L581: As planned in the protocol, PFS of the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was to be compared with that of the defined Investigator's choice of chemotherapy using a stratified log-rank test in the ITT population. The median PFS of both treatment arms was estimated using the KM method. A stratified Cox proportional hazards regression model was also used to estimate the relative hazards of PFS events for patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus the Investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. The hazard ratio and corresponding 97.5% CI were calculated.

L594: ## Patient Disposition
L595: As specified in the protocol, the final analysis for CR/CRi was to be performed after the first 218 patients had been followed for at least 3 months after randomization. Since the 218th patients were randomized to the study on 26 June 2014, the data cutoff date was selected to be 02 October 2014.
L596: A summary of patients disposition is presented in the table below. Of the 279 patients randomized into the study as of the data cutoff, a total of 259 (92.8%) (98.6% patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 87.0% patients in the control arm). A total 3 (2.1%) patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 1 (0.7%) patients in the control arm had completed the treatment. A total of 127 (45.5%) patients (50.4% patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 40.6% patients in the control arm) were ongoing (either still in treatment or on follow-up for disease progression or survival). AEs were reported for 137/141 (97.2%) patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 119/138 (86.2%) patients in the control arm. Of note, considering the ITT population, 20 patients (2 in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 18 in the control arm) were randomized but not treated at the time of the data cutoff. In the safety population, 13 patients randomized to the control arm were randomized but not dosed and were excluded from the safety population.
L597: Of the first 218 patients randomized, 205 (94.0%) patients (100% patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 88.1% patients in the control arm) had received study therapy. A total of 3 (2.8%) patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 1 (0.9%) patient in the control arm had completed the maximum doses of treatment allowed by the protocol.

L612: As specified in the protocol, the final analysis for OS was to be performed after at least 248 OS events, if accrual of 325 patients was achieved. The study completed its enrollment on 04 January 2015 with 326 randomized. A total of 252 OS events were reported in the CRF by 08 March 2016. Therefore, 08 March 2016 was selected as the database cutoff date for results summarized in this submission.
L613: A summary of patient disposition is presented in table below. The ITT population included all 326 patients randomized in the study. In the ITT Population, a total of 307 (94.2%) patients had received study therapy. A total of 10 (6.1%) patients in inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 1 (0.6%) patients in the control arm had completed maximum number of cycles and doses of treatment allowed by the protocol. A total of 23.8% (39/164) patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 9.3% (15/162) patients in the control arm were ongoing (either in follow-up for disease progression or survival). All 164 (100%) patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 143/162 (88.3%) patients in the control arm were analyzed for safety. Of note, 19 patients in the ITT population (0 in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 19 in the control) were randomized but never treated. These patients were excluded from the safety population.

L620: ## **Reviewer's Comment**
L621: *Based on protocol, the definition of completed treatment was patients who completed the maximum number cycles and doses of treatment per protocol. Patients were allowed to receive study treatment for up to 6 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin and for up to 4 cycles of the Investigator's choice of chemotherapy, or 2 cycles of 12 doses of HIDAC. The definition of completed the study was patients who completed survival follow-up for 5 years from randomization of last patient.*

L626: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L627: There were 10 patients with protocol violations regarding eligibility criteria that could potentially compromise the efficacy outcome and the Applicant excluded them from the PP population. Four patients were in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 6 were in the control arm.
L628: *Reviewer Comment: Based upon review of these protocol deviations, the reviewer agrees with the assessment by the Sponsor that these 10 patients with protocol deviations relating to eligibility criteria should be excluded from the per protocol population.*
L629: *None of the major protocol violations would likely bias the study in favor of the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm; all patients were included in the FDA's analysis of efficacy endpoints. Additional efficacy analysis of CR/CRi and OS based on the PP protocol were similar to the findings in the ITT population. The inclusion of the 10 patients does not bias the study in favor of inotuzumab ozogamicin.*

L651: CD22 immunophenotyping was performed on peripheral blood or BM aspirates. CD22 expression was assessed at local study sites for eligibility and at central laboratory. The central laboratory results demonstrated a higher percentage of patients with CD22 expression (≥90%) when compared with the local results for the ITT population.
L652: Reviewer Comment: CD22 is expressed in over &gt;90% of lymphoblasts in patients with ALL (Shah 2015). Testing for CD markers including CD19, CD20, and CD22 is routine in the diagnostic evaluation of patients with ALL. Given the high expression of CD22 in lymphoblasts and that CD22 expression is routinely tested for in the diagnosis of ALL, no restrictions on CD22 expression is required in the UPSI.
L653: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L654: Each dose of study drug and control drug was administered intravenously by study personnel. All study participants received concomitant medications. The most commonly used concomitant medications were antidepressants and anti-anxiety agents, pain medications, and antimicrobials used for infection prophylaxis and treatment. There was more use of antibiotics in the investigator choice arm than the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm.

L657: Source: FDA Analysis ADCM dataset
L658: The use of corticosteroids and other anticancer drugs were allowed to be given within 2 weeks from randomization to the end of Cycle 1 day 5. These agents were allowed to reduce the
L659: number of circulating blasts to &lt; 10,000/uL. Some medications were used as maintenance therapy prior to relapse. During the course of the study, corticosteroids were allowed for CNS prophylaxis(intrathecal chemotherapy), as premedication for up to 1 day, to treat hypersensitivity reactions for up to 1 day and as anti-emetics for up to 8 days/cycle as supportive care. Within the safety population, 94% of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 95(66%) of patients in the control arm receive systemic corticosteroids or other anticancer medications during this time period. Hydroxyurea was administered to 20 patients (12%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 15(11%) in the control arm within 2 weeks from randomization to end of Cycle 1 day 5.
L660: Corticosteroids for premedication, rash or conjunctivitis or CNS prophylaxis used in the inotuzumab arm and control arm respectively: Dexamethasone (38% vs 61%), hydrocortisone (39% vs 24%), methylprednisolone (46% vs 22%). During the study period, more patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm (87.8%) versus 57.5% received systemic steroid treatment.
L661: Hematopoietic growth factor use was used less frequently in the inotuzumab arm compared to the investigator choice arm (67/164, 41%) versus 117/142(82%), respectively.
L662: *Reviewer Comment: There are likely two reasons for discrepancy between corticosteroid use in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the investigator choice arm. The first is that corticosteroids were required as a protocol-specified premedication prior to each dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin. Second, the study duration of therapy for the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was longer compared to the control arm (median of 3 cycles versus 1 cycle).*

L669: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint CR/CRI
L670: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L671: Table 23 below shows summary of primary analyses results for CR/CRI (per EAC) in the ITT218 based on data cutoff of 02 October 2014. The CR/CRI rate per EAC was 80.7% with 95% CI of (72.1, 87.7) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared with 29.4% with 95% CI of (21.0, 38.8) in the control arm. The rate difference was 51.4% with 97.5% CI of (38.4, 64.3) and was statistically significant (1-sided p&lt;0.0001 using Chi-square test). The CR rate was 35.8% in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, and 17.4% in the control arm; the rate difference was 18.3% (97.5% CI: 5.2, 31.5). The CRI rate was 45.0% in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, and 11.9% in the control arm; the rate difference was 33.0% with 97.5% CI of (20.3, 45.8).

L691: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint OS
L692: The first IA of OS was conducted based on data cutoff date of 21 April 2014, after 70 OS events had occurred in 174 randomized patients. The eDMC reviewed the results on 16 June 2014, and recommended the study continue as planned. The second IA of OS was conducted based on data cutoff date of 19 January 2015, when 163 OS events had occurred. The eDMC reviewed the results on 11 March 2015, and recommended the study continue as planned. The efficacy boundary at the final OS analysis was determined based on the timing of 2 IAs in conjunction with the actual number of event at the time of final OS analysis. Since the percentages of deaths were different from the planned percentage at the interim analyses, the actual p-value to reject the null hypothesis (i.e., stop for efficacy at the second interim analysis and the p-values to stop for futility at both interim analyses were based on the alpha (efficacy) and beta (futility) spending functions and the fraction of information. Table below shows the actual stopping boundaries at the interim and final analyses (p-value are 1-sided).
