L706: ## Reviewer's Comments:
L707: A central assumption of Cox regression is that covariate effects on the hazard rate, namely hazard ratio, are constant over time. Violations of the proportional hazard assumption may cause bias in the estimated coefficients as well as incorrect inference regarding significance of effects. If the assumption of proportional hazards holds, the graphs of the survival function should look parallel, in the sense that they should have basically the same shape, should not cross and the curves should not be close in earlier period and then diverge slowly through follow up time. However, Kaplan-Meier estimates of the survival function plotted for OS using ITT population crossed at around 13 months, indicating a violation of the assumption of proportional hazard. The non-proportionality in hazard rates was also examined by plotting the Schoenfeld residuals, as shown in the figure below. significant deviation from zero in the slope of the schoenfeld residuals indicates a violation of the proportional hazard assumption. Further examinations of potential confoundings that led to the non-PH are presented in the following sections.
L708: Figure 12 Schoenfeld Residuals
L709: Table 31 below shows the summary of censoring distributions between two treatment arms. One possible reason that caused the non-proportionality may be the unbalanced censoring distribution between the two treatment groups. There were more early censored observations in the control arm compared with those from Inotuzumab ozogamicin arm.

L715: ## Reviewer's Comments:
L716: Overall survival (OS) is considered to be an important safety endpoint in oncology studies. In the study under discussion, it was observed that the OS result was confounded by imbalance in patients receiving hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HSCT), as well as imbalance in subsequent therapies, transplant related mortality, duration of overall survival follow-up, and censoring. 43.3% of patients in the Inotuzumab ozogamicin treated arm and 11% in the control received HSCT during the course of the treatment. Of note is that the decision to undergo treatment may have depended on the availability of donors, and investigators decisions which may have depended on the treatment responses after induction treatment and/or MRD prior to HSCT. FDA performed the following exploratory analysis to access the impact of the confounding effect on OS due to the stem cell transplant:
L717: - Cox model with time dependent covariate (HSCT) with stratified covariates:The hazard ratio for the treatment effect was 0.81 with 95% CI of (0.64-1.05)
- The hazard ratio for the time dependent covariate of HSCT was 0.50 with 95% CI of (0.35-0.70)
- Cox model including interaction HSCT by treatment:The hazard ratio for treatment effect was 0.72 with 95% CI of (0.55-1.01)
- The hazard ratio for the time dependent covariate of HSCT was 0.34 with 95% CI of (0.20, 0.57)
L718: - Nominal p-value for the treatment by HSCT interaction was 0.04
L719: The figure below shows FDA's exploratory analyses for the association between HSCT and OS (post HSCT). The plot demonstrates that:
L720: - The OS results confounded by HSCT
- No difference seen between treatments in subjects not receiving HSCT
- For those subjects receiving HSCT, there are fewer early deaths in the control arm, but the inotuzumab subjects have a better survival outcome than the control subjects after 18 months.
L721: If HSCT effect is considered in the analysis, we cannot make conclusion about the effect of treatment regarding OS; it is not clear whether the effect on OS is due to the treatment or due to the HSCT.
L722: Clinical Reviewer Comment: Some of these salvage therapies are associated with improved response rate, PFS or OS. The greater use of salvage therapies in the control arm after disease progression or relapse may confound the interpretation of OS. Patients in the Inotuzumab Ozogamicin arm were more likely to proceed directly to a post-study HSCT. There was more post-HSCT related toxicity and mortality including non-relapse mortality in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm (VOD and infectious complications) compared to the control arm. Analysis of OS shows that the patient who benefit the most from inotuzumab ozogamicin were those patients in achieved CR/Cri and MRD negativity and underwent a follow-up HSCT.
L723: Figure 13 Analysis of Association between HSCT and OS
L724: *AVAL=time, Contr N=patient in the control arm without receiving HSCT; Contr Y=patient in the control arm received HSCT; Inotu N=patient in the inotuzumab arm without receiving HSCT; Inotu Y=patient in the inotuzumab arm received HSCT
L725: Table below shows the FDA's exploratory analysis using competing risk analysis which allows separating events by different causes. Death due to relapse or progression, or disease related death, is the event that we are interested, and death due to other cause as competing risk. Using competing risk model, the HR was 0.585 with 97.5% CI of (0.43, 0.79)

L729: - There are more disease related death compared with death due to other cause (competing risk)
L730: - Among patients with disease related death, the Inotuzumab ozogamicin group has a lower event rate compared with control group. In other words, patients have better survival outcome with disease related death in the inotuzumab ozogamicin group.
- Among patients with death due to other cause, the results are reversed. Control arm has better outcome compared with patients in inotuzumab ozogamicin.

L738: Reviewer's comments:
L739: When patients BMA blasts\ge50%, or patients received pre-study transplant, the hazard ratios are great than 1 in favor of control arm. However, the interpretation of those results is difficult since the estimate of the treatment difference for each subgroup almost certainly has lower precision because of smaller sample sizes unless the precision of subgroup estimate has been considered properly in planning the sample size or the variability of the response is sufficiently small in the subgroup. Consequently, the statistical power for detecting the same magnitude of the treatment effect may be insufficient in those subgroup analyses. Therefore, we recommend that those subgroup analysis interpretations are only exploratory and suggestive, and the results are yet to be confirmed by prospective studies of the subgroups.

L768: ## Reviewer's Comments:
L769: The PRO analysis results were generally in favor of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to those in the control arm. However, the distribution of questionnaires is imbalanced between the two arms. For questionnaires of EQRTC QLQ-C30 where patients completed at least 1 question, the overall completion rate was 85.3% for the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 65.3% from the control arm. In addition, in the control arm the number of patients remaining on treatment decreased markedly by Cycle 2 (16.7%), with only 3.1% patients and 0.6% patients remaining by Cycle 3 and Cycle 4, respectively. Due to the imbalanced distribution of missing data between the two arms, the results should be interpreted with caution.

The results were reviewed by the clinical reviewer.

L789: ## Additional MRD Analyses
L790: MRD negativity was defined by flow cytometry as leukemic cells comprising &lt; 1 \times 10^{-4} (&lt;0.01\%) of bone marrow nucleated cells. MRD results were also analyzed based on overall survival and by subjects who attained a CR or CR/CRi. L799: - CRh is defined as ANC&gt;500/microliter and platelet count&gt;50,000/microliter with &lt; 5% blasts in the bone marrow.
- CRi is defined as &lt; 5 blasts in the bone marrow and the absence of peripheral blood leukemic blasts, partial recovery of peripheral blood counts (platelets &lt; 100 \times 10^9/L and ANC&lt;1 \times 10^9/L).
- CR is defined as &lt; 5% blasts in the bone marrow and absence of peripheral blood leukemic blasts, full recovery of peripheral blood counts (platelets \ge 100 \times 10^9/L and ANC \ge 1 \times 10^9/L and resolution of any extramedullary disease).

L813: ## Study B1931010 (Study 1010)
L814: ## Study Design
L815: The principal evidence for the clinical efficacy and safety of inotuzumab ozogamicin is derived from 2 studies of patients with relapsed or refractory B-cell ALL: pivotal phase 3 Study B1931022 and supportive phase 1/2 single-arm Study B1931010. In this section, the study design and efficacy analysis results for the supportive Study B1931010 are briefly summarized.
L816: The Study B1931010 was an open-label, single-arm, multicenter, phase 1/2 study of single agent inotuzumab ozogamicin in adult patients with relapsed or refractory CD22- positive ALL. The objective of the phase 1 dose escalation portion of the study was to identify and characterize the RP2D. The phase 2 single-arm 2-stage portion of the study further evaluated
L817: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L818: the efficacy, safety, and tolerability profile of inotuzumab ozogamicin in adult patients with relapsed or refractory CD22-positive ALL to second or later salvage therapy.
L819: Treatment with inotuzumab ozogamicin was continued until disease progression, patient refusal, unacceptable toxicity, or up to a maximum of 6 cycles, whichever occurred first. Patients received 2 to 3 weekly doses of inotuzumab ozogamicin over a 28-day cycle for a maximum of 6 cycles. Up to 6 dose levels and schedules of administration were planned. If changes were required to the proposed doses, schedules, and PK time points based on emerging data, the protocol was amended accordingly during the study. Approximately 30 to 70 patients were expected to be enrolled.
L820: In the phase 1 dose-finding portion of the study, the exact sample size was not pre-specified in advance since it was an outcome-adaptive Bayesian procedure, but a maximum of 36 patients were to be enrolled. In the phase 1 dose-expansion portion of the study, an additional 12 patients were enrolled to further assess the safety, tolerability, and efficacy of the RP2D.
L821: In the phase 2 portion of the study, approximately 35 patients were to be enrolled. In this portion of the study, the dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin was based on the RP2D and schedule determined in the phase 1 portion of the study. A 2-stage design was used, with 12 patients expected to be treated in the first stage and 23 patients treated in the second stage. For enrollment to proceed to the second stage, at least 2 patients had to achieve a complete response (CR/CRi) in the first stage. At the end of the study, at least 12 complete responses (CR/CRi) in 35 treated patients were required in order to recommend further studies of this dose and schedule in the patient population.
L822: ## Efficacy Results-Phase 2
L823: In the phase 2 study population, the CR/CRi rate was 68.6% (95% CI: 50.7-83.2). The median time to CR/CRi for the 24 patients who achieved CR/CRi was 25.5 days (range: 15 to 91 days). Of the 24 patients who achieved CR or CRi, 18/24 (75%) patients also achieved MRD-negativity and the median time to MRD-negativity was 25.5 days (range: 21-80 days). The median DoR1 (duration of remission [CR/CRi]) was 16.6 weeks (95% CI: 9.4, 25.1) (i.e., 3.8 months [95% CI: 2.2-5.8]). The median PFS was 16.0 weeks (95% CI: 11.3-20.6) (i.e., 3.7 months [95% CI: 2.6-4.7]). The median OS was 27.9 weeks (95% CI: 19.6-34.3) (i.e. 6.4 months [95% CI: 4.5-7.9]). In this late stage salvage setting population, 8/35 (22.9%) patients underwent HSCT after study therapy or subsequent induction therapy.
L824: ## Reviewer's Comments:
L825: (b) (4) (b) (4) this study is a single arm early phase study intended to evaluate activity of the treatment, the analysis results should be considered as exploratory. (b) (4)
L826: Also time to event endpoints such as PFS and OS are not interpretable in a single arm study.
L827: ## 7.2.3. Study 1931008 (Study 1008)
L828: Study 1008 is an open-label, randomized, Phase 3 Study of Inotuzumab Ozogamicin administered in combination with Rituximab Compared to Defined Investigator's Choice Therapy in Subjects with Relapsed or Refractory CD22+ Aggressive NHL who are not candidates for Intensive High-Dose Chemotherapy.
L829: The primary endpoint of Study 1008 was overall survival. The planned sample size was 377 subjects and the study enrolled 322 subjects. At the first interim analysis the median overall survival was (b) (4) months in the Inotuzumab Ozogamicin plus rituximab arm compared to (b) (4) months in the control arm. (b) (4) The study was stopped for futility since p-value (b) (4).
L830: ## 7.2.4. Study 1931006 (Study 1006)
L831: Study 1006 is an open-label, randomized, Phase 3 Study of Inotuzumab Ozogamicin Administered in Combination with Rituximab Compared to a Defined Investigator's Choice Therapy in Subjects with Relapsed or Refractory CD22 Positive Follicular B Cell NHL.
L832: The primary endpoint was progression free survival but only enrolled 29 of planned 978 subjects. The study was terminated early as it was unlikely that the study would ever meet estimated enrollment.
L833: *Reviewer Comment: There is no efficacy data for the use of inotuzumab ozogamicin in patients with Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma. Inclusion of lack of efficacy in subjects with NHL in the USPI is recommended.*
L834: ## 7.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L835: ## 7.3.1. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L836: ## Primary Endpoints
L837: There were two primary efficacy endpoints (CR/CRi and OS) in Study 1022. The study had at least 88.5% power to detect a difference of response probabilities between the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus the control arm. In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, the primary efficacy endpoint of CR/CRi was attained in 81% subjects (95% CI: 72%, 88%) vs 29% subjects (95% CI: 21%, 39%) in the control arm for a rate difference of 51% with 1-sided p &lt; 0.0001 based on the primary analysis of the initial 218 randomized patients. This improvement was consistent when evaluating the ITT population (all 326 patients) for CR/CRi [73% (95% CI: 66%, 80%) in inotuzumab ozogamicin arm vs 31% (24-39)] in the control arm, rate difference 42% with 1-sided p &lt; 0.0001. For the primary analysis of overall survival in the ITT population the estimated hazard ratio for overall survival was 0.77 (97.5% CI 0.58, 1.03) with one- sided p-
L838: 0.0203 for the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the control arm in Study 1022. This difference did not reach statistical significance as it exceeded the prespecified 1-sided p-value boundary of 0.0104. The number of patients who underwent follow-on HSCT was 77/164(47%) in the inotuzumab arm compared to 19/33(58%) in the control arm. The overall post study therapy HSCT mortality was 60% (46/164) in the inotuzumab arm and 58% (19/162) in the control arm and the post study therapy HSCT non-relapse mortality was 38%(29/77) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus 24%(8/33) in the control arm.
L839: The efficacy results from Study 1010 are comparable to study 1022 noting that subjects in Study 1010 were more heavily pretreated with 78% in Salvage 2 or greater. In study 1010, the CR/CRi rate was 68% (95% CI 56, 78.6) for all 72 patients.
L840: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L841: In the pivotal study, improvement was consistent for all secondary endpoints. In particular rates of MRD negativity in patients who achieved CR/CRi were higher in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the chemotherapy arm based on the ITT population [77%(95% CI: 74%,88%)] vs [38%(95% CI:26%,43%)] for a rate difference of 50% with 1-sided p &lt; 0.0001). The rate of MRD negativity in patients in patients who received inotuzumab ozogamicin and who achieved a CR was 90% (95% CI: 76%- 97%) compared with the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm of 32% (95% CI: 13%- 57%) in the ITT218 population. The subjects who attained CR/CRi and MRD negativity and with subsequent HSCT, the estimated median overall survival was 17.8 months vs patients with CR/CRi and MRD positivity and subsequent HSCT with an estimated median overall survival of 11.9 months. It is unclear if this longer survival is due to MRD status or treatment effect of inotuzumab.
L842: In the ITT 218 population, 45%(49/109) patients attained a CRi(EAC assessment) and of these patients 34/49(69.4%) attained MRD negativity. In the investigator choice of chemotherapy arm, 11.9%(13/109) achieved a CRi with only 3/13(23%) attaining MRD negativity. In the inotuzumab arm, the median duration of response for patients who attained a CRi was 4.6 months (95% CI 3.7,5.7) compared to investigator choice arm of median duration of response of 2.9 months with 95% CI(0.6, 5.7). In the ITT population, (investigator assessment), there were 65/164(39.6%) patients with CRi compared with 24/162(14.8%) patients with CRi in the control arm.
L843: Based on the data from this randomized study, the CRi responders performed better overall with respect to MRD response and duration of response compared to the chemotherapy control arm. In patients with relapsed ALL in CR2, MRD may be a more powerful predictor of outcome than CRp or CRi with regard to relapse free survival and patients with MRD positivity are likely to have higher risk of relapse (Saygin 2017). HSCT in CR2 also appears to be an independent prognostic predictor for OS and RFS (Saygin 2017). The depth and magnitude of response for patients who attained a CRi with MRD negativity provides additional support for the inclusion of the composite CR(CR and CRi).
L844: ## Subpopulations
L845: The improvement in CR/CRI was consistent across all prespecified stratification subgroups (duration of first remission, line of salvage and age at randomization).
L846: ## 7.3.2. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L847: The efficacy of inotuzumab ozogamicin in relapsed or refractory ALL has been established, primarily on the results of a single randomized, phase III study in patients greater than or equal to 18 years of age with relapsed or refractory ALL in which patients (ITT218 population) who received inotuzumab ozogamicin had better complete response and complete responses with incomplete hematological recovery (CR/CRI) 81% (95% CI: 72%, 88%) compared to the patients who received a defined investigator's choice of chemotherapy (29%, 95% CI: 21%, 39%). The responses were consistent in analysis of the full ITT population and essentially consistent across the subpopulations tested.
L848: The secondary endpoints of CR and duration of response were used to inform the regulatory decision making process. CR was achieved by 39/109 (36%) subjects (95% CI: 27%, 46%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm vs 19/109 (17%) subjects (95% CI: 11%, 26%) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. The response was durable with estimated median duration of complete response of 8 months (95% CI: 5, 10 months) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to 5 months (95% CI: 3, 7 months) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm.
L849: Subjects on Study 1022 were tested for MRD using flow cytometry and MRD levels less than &lt;1 \times 10^{-4} are expected to be reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit in the intended population. In patients who received inotuzumab ozogamicin and attained a CR, a reduction in MRD to less than 10^{-4} was achieved by 90% (95% CI: 76%, 97%) compared to the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm of 32% (95% CI: 13%, 57%) in the ITT218 population. For patients who achieved CR/CRI in the ITT population (n=326), a reduction in MRD to less than 10^{-4} was achieved by 92/120 (78%) subjects (95% CI: 68%, 87%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus 19/50 (38%) subjects (95% CI: 24%, 47%) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. An FDA exploratory analysis of the overall survival based on response as assessed by EAC and MRD negativity was performed. The depth of response for patients who attained either a CR or CRI with MRD negativity provides additional support for the inclusion of the MRD negativity rate for patients who attained a CRI. Based on the data Study 1022, the CRI responders performed better overall with respect to MRD response and duration of response compared to the patients in the chemotherapy control arm who attained a CRI. In the inotuzumab arm, the patients with CRI appear to be comparable to patients who attained CR with regard to MRD negativity and durability of response.
L850: In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, the rate of attainment of CRh (29%) in the ITT218 population per EAC is comparable to the rate of CR (35.8%) and CRI (45.0%). The magnitude of difference between the attainment of CRh in the inotuzumab arm (29%) compared to the investigator choice arm (5%) is notable as is the rate of MRD negativity for the CRh (71.8%)
L851: versus 20%(inotuzumab vs investigator choice arm). The patients with CRh appear to be comparable to patients who attained a CR with regard to response and MRD negativity.
L852: An improvement in the second primary endpoint of overall survival did not reach statistical significance as it exceeded the prespecified 1-sided p-value boundary of 0.0104 in the pivotal phase 3 study (B1931022). The estimated hazard ratio for the primary endpoint of OS in the ITT population was 0.770 (97.5% CI: 0.578, 1.026) with an estimated median OS of 7.7 months (95% CI 6, 9.2) for inotuzumab ozogamicin versus 6.7 months (95% CI 4.9 8.3) in the control arm with one- sided p=0.0203. For subjects who received HSCT, there were fewer early deaths in the control arm however subjects in the inotuzumab arm have better survival outcome than the control subjects after 18 months (p-value for treatment effect by HSCT interaction &lt;0.05). In the competing risk analysis when death was attributed to disease only, there are fewer cumulative deaths over time in the inotuzumab arm than the control arm. When considering deaths attributed to causes other than disease, the reverse was true, there were more cumulative deaths over time in the inotuzumab arm compared to the control arm (non-relapse mortality in inotuzumab arm of 38%(29/77) versus 24%(8/33) in the control arm).
L853: The higher non-relapse mortality in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm is due primarily to the occurrence of hepatic VOD and resulting complications in patients who proceeded to subsequent HSCT after inotuzumab ozogamicin. The increased risk of post-HSCT non-relapse mortality in patients with relapsed or refractory ALL who receive inotuzumab ozogamicin and proceed to subsequent HSCT warrants a boxed warning in the product label.
L854: **In summary**, the current standard of care for the treatment of relapsed or refractory ALL is intensive combination chemotherapy. The CR rate of 36% with a durable response (median 8 months) are better than the responses for the control arm of defined investigator choices of single agent or combination chemotherapy regimens. Moreover, an MRD response was noted in 78% of patients who attained a CR/CRI compared to only 38% in the chemotherapy arm. Although inotuzumab ozogamicin failed to reach statistical significance for overall survival, subjects in the inotuzumab arm had better survival outcome than the control subjects after 18 months. In the inotuzumab arm, for patients who proceeded to transplantation, the early deaths and worse non-relapse mortality were due to VOD. Taken together, the efficacy outcomes (CR, durability, and MRD) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm form a strong basis for a conclusion of effectiveness provided that the safety risks (VOD, increase in non-relapse mortality) are adequately described in the labeling information.
L855: Given the prevalence of CD22 expression on lymphoblasts in patients with ALL and the routine testing for CD22 expression in patients with ALL, no restriction on CD22 + expression is recommended. The pivotal study included patients with Philadelphia chromosome positive ALL(Ph+ ALL) and there appears to be no difference in response for patients with Ph+ ALL versus those with Philadelphia chromosome negative ALL and thus the indication includes all patients with relapsed or refractory precursor B-cell ALL. Data from studies 1006 and 1008 in subjects with NHL demonstrates no evidence for efficacy and the prescribing information
L856: should include statement regarding the lack of efficacy for inotuzumab ozogamicin in patients with NHL.
L857: ## 7.4. Review of Safety
L858: ## Safety Review Approach
L859: The clinical reviewer confirmed the Applicant's safety analyses from the 2 studies in patients with relapsed or refractory B-Cell ALL: the pivotal study B1931022 and supportive Study B1931010. The clinical reviewer also reviewed the safety analysis performed by the Applicant from 9 studies in patients with relapsed or refractory non-Hodgkin lymphoma (NHL) treated with inotuzumab ozogamicin as a single agent, in combination with rituximab, or in combination with rituximab plus chemotherapy. The analyses were conducted primarily using MedDRA Adverse event Diagnosis Service (MAED) and JMP statistical programs.
L860: A key safety issue with inotuzumab ozogamicin identified *a priori* is hepatic veno-occlusive (VOD) disease or sinusoidal obstructive syndrome (SOS). There is a known safety issue with the use of a related drug (Mylotarg) and the risk for development of hepatic VOD. Particular attention was given to hepatotoxicity and in particular hepatic VOD/SOS during this review.
L861: ## 7.4.2. Review of the Safety Database

L863: A total of 880 patients have received at least one dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin in applicant sponsor studies. This safety review focuses on the comparison of the 164 patients who received inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to the investigator choice (N=143) in Study 1022. In addition, the pooled R/R ALL population consists of the 164 patients from Study 1022 and 48 patients who received the proposed dose (of inotuzumab ozogamicin from study 1010. The median number of cycles was 3 in the inotuzumab arm and 1 in the control arm.

L892: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L893: The size of the safety database is adequate to provide a reasonable estimate of adverse reactions that may be observed with inotuzumab ozogamicin and the duration of treatment is adequate to allow assessments of adverse reactions over time. Study 1022 and the pooled R/R populations are representative of typical patients with R/R ALL that participate on clinical trials. However, non-white and Hispanic patients are under-represented compared to the overall ALL population in the United States.
L894: ## 7.4.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L895: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L896: This submission was of adequate quality for clinical review. Overall, the submission was well-organized with appropriate analyses and detailed reports and summaries. There are no concerns regarding the integrity of the submission.
L897: A data fitness assessment was performed in collaboration with the Office of Computational Science (OCS). The data fitness review included assessments of data fitness for Study B1931022 and Study B1931010 including but not limited to: availability of appropriate variables, variables populated by expected data points, appropriate use of standard terminology, data well described by metadata. There were no major issues regarding data integrity identified during the course of the review.
L898: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L899: Adverse events (AE) were reported down to the verbatim term. The adverse events were coded using MedDRA version 18.1 coding dictionary and V 3.0 Common Terminology Criteria Adverse (CTCAE) events. For Study 1010, MedDRA version 17.1 coding dictionary and V 3.0 CTCAE criteria were applied. The safety data cut-off date for Study 1022 is March 8, 2016. For Study 1010, the safety data cut-off is January 30, 2015. For the pooled R/R ALL studies, March 8, 2016 safety cut-off date was used for study 1022 and for Study 1010 the safety data cut-off date was
L900: January 15, 2016. The FDA compared the verbatim adverse event term with the MedDRA preferred term for all adverse events reported in Study 1022 and did not identify any irregularities.
L901: The definition of treatment-emergent adverse events for the all studies (Study 1022 and Study 101) includes all causality AEs that began on or after Cycle 1 Day 1 but within 42 days of the last dose, any treatment-related AEs that began on or any time after C1D1, and any VOD event that began within 2 years after the randomization date regardless of attribution to study therapy. For the NHL studies, all causality AEs that began on or after C1D1 but within 56 days after the last dose and VOD event that began at any time on or after C1D1 regardless of casual attribution to study therapy. The treatment emergent adverse event definition for the adverse drug reaction for the proposed labeling is defined as: all causality AEs that commenced on after C1D1 within 42 days after the last dose but prior to new anti-cancer treatment (including (b) (4) HSCT).
L902: *Reviewer Comment: The Applicant applied a different definition for ADRs to identify events considered by the Applicant to be at least partially related to single-agent inotuzumab ozogamicin excluding the effect of subsequent anti-cancer therapy. This reviewer agrees that this approach likely captures adverse events at least partially related to single-agent inotuzumab ozogamicin however all events of VOD should be included in this analysis.*
L903: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L904: Clinical laboratory assessments were performed as specified in each individual protocol with routine blood tests performed at least every 4 weeks. In study 1022, laboratory abnormalities were to be reported as AEs if meeting any of the following criteria:
L905: - Accompanied by clinical symptoms
- Results in medical intervention
- Results in change of study treatment(e.g., dosage administration, treatment interruption or treatment discontinuation)
- Clinically significant in the investigator's judgement.
L906: The safety assessment methods and time points described in the protocols seem adequate for the population, disease and indication being investigated.

L940: The grade 5 VOD events occurred after study treatment and subsequent HSCT. The subject who died of multi-organ failure died 133 days after the last dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin but had ongoing grade 4 VOD/SOS at time of death which occurred after HSCT. The following narratives describe the 5 patients who died due to VOD/SOS.
L941: **Subject 10081002:** Patient is a 46 year-old male who received 4 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin and developed grade 2 hyperbilirubinemia and attained a MRD negative CRI after Cycle 1. He proceeded to HSCT (mMUD, CY/TBI conditioning regimen) and developed CMV infection, multi-organ failure and Grade 4 VOD 16 days after HSCT. No defibrotide was used. Fatal VOD occurred Day + 46 post HSCT and 91 days after last study dose. HEAB assessed second liver event as VOD/SOS.
L942: **Subject 10141007:** Patient is a 29 year-old female who received 3 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin and achieved CRI after Cycle 1. She had prestudy HSCT(MUD: Cy/TBI) then relapsed prior to enrollment and went on to receive 3 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin. She subsequently underwent a second HSCT (mMUD, CY/BU/CLO) and developed grade 4 VOD 38 days post-HSCT. Fatal VOD occurred 94 days after HSCT. HEAB assessed event as definite VOD.
L943: **Subject 11511010:** Patient is a 23 year-old male who received 3 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin and proceeded to HSCT(MUD, BU/FLU/thiotepa/TBI conditioning therapy) and developed fungal pneumonia and Grade 2 VOD 6 days after HSCT. The patient did achieve CRI after Cycle 1. HEAB assessed as possible severe VOD.
L944: **Subject 12111001:** The patient is a 65 year-old male who received 2 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin and achieved a CRI. He proceeded to HSCT (RIC: flu/MEL) and developed grade 4 VOD 2 months post-HSCT. He received defibrotide for treatment of VOD. Fatal VOD occurred 89 days after HSCT and patient died VOD per investigator. HEAB assessment was that liver biopsy was more consistent with intrahepatic cholestatic disease. HEAB assessed as no evidence of VOD in material reviewed.
L945: **Subject 12721004:** The patient is a 42 year-old female who received 2 cycles of inotuzumab ozogamicin and attained a CRI. The patient had prior history of 2 previous HSCT. The patient underwent a third MUD and died 21 days after HSCT. Per report, patient developed Grade 3 VOD (treated with defibrotide) as well as disseminated mucormycosis. HEAB assessed as VOD. VOD was noted in autopsy as well as disseminated mucormycosis.
L946: **Reviewer Comment:** In the inotuzumab arm, there were 12 deaths between Cycle 1 Day 1 and 42 days after the last dose of study that were not reported as grade 5 AEs. All of these deaths occurred more than 28 days after the last dose of study drug and did not require reporting as AES per the protocol. Of these 12 deaths, 6 were due to disease under the study and 6 were due to: CNS hemorrhage and disease progression, sepsis and pulmonary infection in setting of disease progression, pseudomonas sepsis, multi-organ failure and disease under the study, pneumonia and disease under the study, cerebral hemorrhage and disease under the study and disease progression and cellulitis.
L947: In the control arm there were 3 deaths between Day 1 and Day 42 after last dose of study drug that were not reported as Grade 5 AEs. All 3 deaths were due to disease under the study occurring beyond 28 days after last dose of study drug.
L948: ## **Deaths within 30 days of Cycle 1 Day 1 (safety population) in Study 1022**
L949: There were 9/164(6%) deaths in the inotuzumab arm compared to 8/143(6%) in the control arm that occurred within 30 days of Cycle 1 in Study 1022. Death due to disease under study occurred in 4 patients (2%) in the inotuzumab arm and 3 patients(2%) in the control arm. The other causes for death in the inotuzumab included hemorrhage from GI bleed (1 subject) and infectious etiologies (4 subjects). In the control arm, the other causes of death included multi-organ failure, infections, disseminated intravascular coagulopathy and tumor lysis syndrome.
L950: ## **Additional Studies**
L951: In the NHL studies, grade 5 SAEs were reported in 45/644(7%) in all the inotuzumab treated patients in the NHL studies. In the pooled single agent NHL studies 9/173(5%) had a grade 5 TEAE.

L954: patients (20%) went on to follow-up HSCT and of these 19/33(57%) had post-transplant mortality.
L955: Post-transplant non-relapse mortality was adjusted for competing risks with competing risk defined as death due to relapse from CR/CRI. The number of patients with post-transplant non-relapse mortality was 29(38%) patients in the inotuzumab arm compared to 8(24%) in the control arm. L969: ## Study 1010
L970: There were a total of 54 deaths (54/72, 75%). Of these 54 deaths, 33 were disease under study due to disease progression (46%) and none were assessed as study drug toxicity. Within the active treatment phase of the study there were 12 deaths (17%) with 8 due to disease
L971: progression. There were 42 deaths after active treatment phase with 25 deaths due to disease progression. One patient had VOD and died. There were 24 patients who proceeded to HSCT after inotuzumab ozogamicin treatment and 16(67%) were alive on Day + 100 and 7 patients (29%) died prior to Day + 100. There were 7 patients who died prior to day + 100 and of these, 6 died due to sepsis/septic shock and VOD in 1 patient and one patient with unknown cause of death.
L972: In the Pooled ALL studies, 88 patients (42%) of 212 underwent follow-up HSCT and of these patients 53(60%) had post-transplant death and 35(40%) had non-relapse mortality.
L973: ## Narratives for all patients who died on Study 1022
L974: The Applicant provided at the request of the Agency efficacy narratives for all patients who died in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and the control arm in Study B1930122 with a March 8, 2016 data cut-off date. The purpose of the efficacy narrative summaries and analysis was to provide further understanding of how the treatment effect of inotuzumab ozogamicin impacts CR/CRI but does not translate into similar magnitude of effect on overall survival for patients who received inotuzumab ozogamicin.
L975: Overall, there a total of 252 deaths in Study 1022 with 122 in the inotuzumab arm and 130 in the control arm. The most common cause of death for patients who achieved CR/CRI to study therapy for both treatment arms was progressive disease [inotuzumab ozogamicin arm 47/81(58%) and control arm 31/40(78%)]. The other causes of death amount the 121 patients who achieved CR/CRI after study therapy includes sepsis, GVHD, pneumonia, HSCT complications, study drug toxicity, respiratory failure, acute respiratory distress syndrome, and multi-organ failure.
L976: In the patients who died, VOD was reported in 21/122(17%) of patients in the inotuzumab arm compared to 2/130(2%) in the control arm. Six of the 122(5%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm had hepatic disease as cause of death compared to 2/130(2%) in the control arm and 20/122(16%) of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm had complications of hepatic disease at the time death compared to 7/130(5%) in the control arm.
L977: Deaths due to infections occurred in 28/122(23%) of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to 18/130(14%) of patients in the control arm. These events may have occurred during study therapy or after follow-up therapy. Of the 68 patients that proceeded to HSCT after study therapy, infection as reported a cause of death in 12/48(25%) in the inotuzumab arm and 3/20(15%) in the control arm. When looking at cause of death and mechanism of death plus major infectious complications, 58/122(48%) of deaths were associated with infections in the inotuzumab arm compared to 48/130(37%) in the control arm.
L978: Deaths due to hemorrhage as primary cause of death but mechanism of death plus major hemorrhagic complications at time of death 18/122(15%) of deaths in the inotuzumab arm were due this compared to 5/130(4%) in the control arm.
L979: GVHD was reported as cause of death in 7/122(6%) in the inotuzumab arm compared to 2/30(2%) in the control arm.
L980: Overall, most patients died in this study due to relapsed or progressive disease with 87/122(71%) of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm dying due to progressive disease and 109/130(84%) in the control arm.
L981: Among the patients who achieved CR/CRi who proceeded to transplantation, 16/41(39%) in the inotuzumab arm and 3/15(20%) in the control arm were in remission at the time of death. The substantial toxicity for patients who proceeded to follow-up HSCT was ongoing or fatal hepatic VOD often in setting of infectious complications.
L982: Patients in the control arm received more subsequent salvage therapy for ALL after failure to respond to treatment or after relapsing. Patients in the inotuzumab arm were more likely to proceed to HSCT whereas patients in the control arm were more likely to proceed to new induction/salvage therapy. Among the patients who died, 45% in the inotuzumab arm and 69% of patients in the control arm received at least 1 salvage therapy. Salvage therapies included blinatumomab, inotuzumab, tyrosine kinase inhibitors, CAR-T cell therapy.
L983: *Reviewer comment: For patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin, outside of disease progression, VOD and infectious complications were the most common causes of death with infectious complications occurring in patients who did and did not proceed to transplantation. In patients who proceeded to subsequent post-study transplantation, VOD or multi-organ failure in setting of VOD was cause of death. Often there were ongoing infectious complications occurring at the same time as VOD or MOF. A boxed warning for increased frequency of VOD and complications of VOD to include multi-organ failure should be included in the USPI.*
L984: ## Serious Adverse Events
L985: An SAE occurring within 42 days of the last dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin was reported for 120 subjects in the pooled R/R ALL population, including 84 subjects in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm in Study 1022. The distribution of SAEs by SOC is shown in Table 63.
L986: **Table 64 All Causality Serious Adverse Events in ≥ 2% of Patients**
L987: System Organ Class

L990: There were 51 patients(31%) in the inotuzumab arm in Study 1022 that developed an SAE considered at least possibly related to inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to 42(29%) in the control arm. The most common treatment related SAE (&gt; 5%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was VOD/SOS in 20 patients (12%), febrile neutropenia in 13 patients (8%). In the control arm, the most common treatment related SAEs were febrile neutropenia in 24 patients(17%), neutropenic sepsis and sepsis with 4 patients each(3% each).
L991: *Reviewer Comment: Overall there was a similar number of SAEs between the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and the investigator choice arm in Study 1022. There were more SAEs in the*
L992: *SOC of hepatobiliary disorders for the inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to the investigator's choice arm with VOD/SOS being source of the difference. There were more SAEs within the blood and lymphatic system SOC in the investigator choice arm compared to inotuzumab arm. The pattern of SAEs observed in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm is consistent with safety signal for hepatotoxicity associated with this drug.*

L998: ## Study 1022
L999: More patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm experienced treatment emergent adverse events that led to permanent treatment discontinuation compared to the Investigator Choice arm. The most common TEAEs associated with treatment discontinuation in the inotuzumab arm were pneumonia (3%), thrombocytopenia (2%), elevated AST, GGT increased(1%), VOD/SOS (1%). There was one patient in the inotuzumab arm who permanently discontinued study drug that occurred 42 days after the start of study treatment due to grade 2 hyperbilirubinemia.
L1000: ## Dose Delays and Dose Reductions
L1001: In Study 1022, there were 72/164(44%) treatment delays in the inotuzumab arm (\ge 5\% of patients) compared to 17/143(12%) in the control arm. In the inotuzumab arm, the most common TEAES by preferred term leading to treatment delays were neutropenia (17%), thrombocytopenia (10%), febrile neutropenia (5%), hyperbilirubinemia (4%), AST increase(4%), GGT increase(4%), ALT increase(3%). In the control arm, there were no single associated treatment emergent adverse events that lead to treatment delays in \ge 5\% of patients.
L1002: In Study 1022, very few patients in either treatment arm required dose reductions due to adverse events. In the inotuzumab arm, 5/164 (3%) of patients required dose reductions and 3/143(&lt;1%) of patients in the control arm required dose reductions. The most common reason for
L1003: dose was reductions was neutropenia (1.2%) in the inotuzumab arm and infection (&lt;1%) in the control arm.
L1004: *Reviewer Comment: One possible explanation for the difference in AEs associated with treatment delays is the number of cycles received by patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the control arm. More patients in the control arm only received 1 cycle of treatment compared to the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. Another difference in AEs leading to treatment delays is the dosing differences of the arms. Inotuzumab ozogamicin was given on weekly schedule compared to 5-7 days during one cycle and thus more likely to have dose delays with weekly schedule.*

L1032: The most frequently reported TEAEs in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm were thrombocytopenia and neutropenia (49% and 29%, respectively) compared to the control arm (61% and 46%). The other frequently reported (≥20%) TEAEs in the inotuzumab arm were anemia (34%), nausea (32%), pyrexia (32%), leukopenia (29%), headache (27%), febrile neutropenia (27%), fatigue (26%), AST increase (23%), GGT increase and hyperbilirubinemia (23% each). In the control arm the most common TEAEs were anemia (55%), febrile neutropenia (54%), nausea (48%), pyrexia (42%), diarrhea (39%), constipation (24%) and hypokalemia (23%).
L1033: The most frequently reported Grade 3 or 4 events in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm were neutropenia and thrombocytopenia (47% and 41%) vs the control arm (44% vs 59%). The other grade 3 and 4 TEAEs reported in \ge 5\% of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin were febrile neutropenia and leukopenia (27% each), anemia (23%), lymphopenia (17%), GGT increased (11%), VOD (8%), hypokalemia (7%) and hyperbilirubinemia and WBC count decreased (6% each).
L1034: *Reviewer Comment: Most of the common TEAEs were lower in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the investigator choice arm. The TEAEs that were reported with a frequency of \ge 10\% and &gt; 10\% absolute difference in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm were AST increase (23% vs 11%), GGT increase (21% vs 8%), and VOD (13% vs &lt; 1\%). These findings are consistent with the increased risk of hepatotoxicity with the use of inotuzumab ozogamicin.*
L1035: *TEAEs with a frequency of \ge 10\% that were reported less often in the inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to the investigator arm (&gt; 10\% absolute difference) include thrombocytopenia (49% vs 61%), anemia (24% vs 55%), febrile neutropenia (27% vs 54%), nausea (32% versus 48%), pyrexia (32% vs 42%), diarrhea (18% vs 39%), rash (9% vs 19%) and mucosal inflammation (4% vs 14%).*

L1043: ## Adverse Drug Reactions (ADRs)
L1044: ADRs were identified by the Applicant based on whether they were reasonably associated with inotuzumab ozogamicin treatment. Preferred terms that were considered to have similar meaning were grouped together (e.g. neutropenia includes PT of neutropenia and neutrophil count decreased). All causality AEs for ADRs were defined as those that stated on or after cycle 1 but within 42 days after the last dose but prior to new anti-cancer treatment. VOD that occurred after follow-up HSCT is not included in the ADR table. This reviewer was able to verify
L1045: the ADR for Study 1022.

L1056: 8- Hypokalemia
L1057: 9- Headache- headache, migraine and sinus headache
L1058: 10- I hemorrhage- hemorrhage terms excluding lab terms but includes MedDRA Version 18.1 Standard MedDRA query(narrow) for Hemorrhage terms(refer to appendix)
L1059: 11- Abdominal pain: abdominal pain, abdominal pain upper, abdominal pain lower, abdominal tenderness, esophageal pain and hepatic pain

L1062: 14- Fatigue- asthenia and fatigue
L1063: 15- Edema- peripheral edema, peripheral swelling

L1067: The most common (\ge 20\% ADRs) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm were thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, infection, anemia, leukopenia, fatigue, hemorrhage, pyrexia, nausea, headache, febrile neutropenia, transaminase increased, abdominal pain, GGT increased and hyperbilirubinemia.
L1068: *Reviewer Comment: The Applicant is proposing to use the ADR with incidence \ge 10\% in*
L1069: *inotuzumab ozogamicin arm in the USPI to describe the adverse event profile. The grouping of the MedDRA PTs is acceptable as it provides a more accurate description of adverse events. The most common ADRs are similar to the most common TEAEs described in the prior sections above. The use of the ADR in the USPI is reasonable as it accurately describes the adverse events.*

L1097: ## Hy's Law
L1098: The Sponsor performed an analysis for Hy's Law cases and used a standard definition of Hy's Law case was applied. In Study 1022, all potential Hy's law cases as well as all cases of elevated bilirubin greater than 2mg/dL(independent of other laboratory abnormalities) were assessed by the HEAB to identify possible drug induced liver injury. In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, 13 cases were reported to be DILI by the HEAB (5 probable, 8 possible). None of these cases were considered to represent possible Hy's law cases except in one patient. In this patient both AST
L1099: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L1100: and ALT values were elevated prior to dosing, elevation of bilirubin and AST were concomitant and the case did not meet Hy's law criteria since alkaline phosphate was also elevated. The definition of DILI by the HEAB did not require that the laboratory criteria for Hy's law be met but only that the liver-related abnormalities observed were thought to be secondary to drug based on their clinical judgment.
L1101: In Study 1022, the Sponsor identified two patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 4 patients in the control arm with concomitant elevations of ALT or AST (AST/ALT &gt; 3 x ULN and total bilirubin &gt; 2 ULN) while on treatment. AP was elevated in 4 of 6 of these cases. None of these cases met the criteria for true Hy's law since liver dysfunction was attributed to overwhelming infection and/or alkaline phosphate elevation.
L1102: The Applicant, based on the analysis described above, did not identify any confirmed Hy's Law cases in study 1022 or 1010. In the single agent NHL studies, there were no confirmed Hy's law cases. In the combination NHL studies, in particular, Study 1003[escalating doses of inotuzumab ozogamicin in combination with rituximab plus chemotherapy( rituximab, cyclophosphamide, vincristine, and prednisone or rituximab, gemcitabine and/or cisplatin and dexamethasone)], there were 2 Hy's law cases confirmed as probable or definite. Additionally, the HEAB from Study 1022 reviewed these cases and confirmed Hy's law. The Applicant provided brief narratives and the FDA reviewer reviewed the cases and agreed with the Applicant's findings.

L1116: Using the preferred term of hypertension in the inotuzumab arm, there were 9 patients (6%) who had hypertension with 2(1%) grade 3 events. In the control arm, there were 9(6%) adverse events of hypertension with 2(1%) grade 3 events. There were no grade\ge 4 events of hypertension in either arm. In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, there were 12(7%) adverse events of hypotension and 1(&lt;1%) grade 3 event. In the investigator choice arm there were 24(17%) adverse events of hypotension with 3(2%) grade 3 and 3(2%) grade 4 events.
L1117: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1118: ECGs were performed at baseline, throughout treatment and at the end of study visit. TEAEs related to ECG changes were reviewed along with all cardiac adverse events. No patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm had QTcF \ge 500msec. One patient in the control arm had QTcF \ge 500msec.
L1119: Increases from baseline occurred in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm (89%) compared to the control arm (73%) with 4/162(3%) of patients in the inotuzumab arm and 3/124(2%) in the investigator choice arm having a maximum QTcF increase from baseline \ge 60msec. None of these patients had any reported ventricular cardiac event associated with QTc prolongation. No patients in the inotuzumab arm displayed any maximum CTCAE toxicity grade 3 or 4 events related to QTcF.
L1120: In study 1022, adverse events due to QT prolongation are defined as a group of all reported preferred terms by using the MedDRA Version 18.1 SMQ Torsade's de pointes/QT prolongation or reports of cardiac fibrillation, seizure, loss of consciousness, sudden cardiac death, sudden death, syncope, ventricular arrhythmia, ventricular fibrillation, ventricular flutter and ventricular tachyarrhythmia. There were 5(3%) QTc prolongation adverse events in patients who received inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to 3(2%) in the investigator control arm. There were no grade 4 or 5 events. In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, there were 3(2%) ECG QT prolongation events, 1(&lt;1%) syncope event and 1(&lt;1%) ventricular tachycardia event. The event of syncope and ventricular tachycardia were not associated with QT interval prolongation. There was one case of fatal cardiac arrest that occurred which was secondary to post-operative intra-abdominal bleeding and no QTc prolongation was reported.
L1121: ## QT
L1122: The FDA Interdisciplinary Review Team (IRT) for QT studies reviewed the thorough QT study. This included the study report for Study 1022 and the electronic datasets. The following summary findings were taken from the IRT review, "Inotuzumab ozogamicin is an antibody-drug conjugate and QTc prolongation is generally not expected for large molecules like inotuzumab ozogamicin, however, prolongation of the QTc interval was observed in multiple clinical studies (Table 1). The observed QTc prolongation does not appear to be due to direct inhibition of the hERG potassium current based on the preclinical data, and the mechanism for the observed QTc prolongation is currently unknown".
L1123: Refer to the interdisciplinary review team consult review for additional details.
L1124: ## Immunogenicity
L1125: In Study 1022, in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, 7/164(4%) patients developed a positive ADA response. Neutralizing antibodies were not detected in any of the tested samples from the patients. Five of the 7 patients who had a positive ADA response had adverse events. Two patients had positive ADA response after treatment with inotuzumab ozogamicin included one patient who developed chills and pyrexia (Grade 1) and anemia (grade 3) and a second patient who developed pyrexia (grade 2). Three patients had positive ADA response prior to treatment with inotuzumab ozogamicin and adverse events included hyperhidrosis and dyspnea (grade 1), intermittent pyrexia, anemia (grade 3) and maculopapular rash (grade 3), hot flush (grade 1) and headaches. There was 1(&lt;1%) AEs of hypersensitivity in the inotuzumab arm (N=164) and 1/143(&lt;1%) in the investigator choice arm.
L1126: *Reviewer Comment: The incidence of hypersensitivity reactions was very small.*

L1136: *Reviewer Comment: There most frequently reported hepatotoxicity adverse events in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm (≥ 5%) compared to the control arm were AST increased (23% vs 11%), GGT increased (21% vs 8%), hyperbilirubinemia (21% vs 17%), ALT increase (15% vs 13%), Blood ALP (13% vs 7%) and VOD (13% vs &lt;1%). The most frequently (≥ 5%) reported ≥ Grade 3 hepatotoxicity adverse events in the inotuzumab arm was GGT increase(11%) and VOD (11%) Hepatotoxicity to include VOD is an important identified risk associated with the use of inotuzumab ozogamicin. Recommend box warning on the USPI for hepatotoxicity and VOD.*

L1141: In the univariate analysis there was an association(2 sided p-value &lt; 0.05) between prior HSCT, salvage therapy status, prior history of liver disease/hepatitis, hepatic impairment at baseline, and peripheral blast count with grade ≥ 3 hepatotoxicity.
L1142: *Reviewer Comment: Based upon the univariate analysis there is an association between prior HSCT, salvage status, prior history of liver disease/hepatitis and hepatic impairment at baseline as well as peripheral blast count with increased risk of grade ≥ 3 hepatotoxicity.*
L1143: ## VOD
L1144: Hepatic VOD is a serious and life threatening complication of HSCT. In Studies 1022 and 1010, all cases of VOD for up to 2 years after randomization regardless of causality, severity or treatment arm were reported by the investigators. All cases of VOD were reported as SAEs.
L1145: Hepatotoxicity to include VOD has been reported for antibody-drug conjugates which contain calicheamicin to include inotuzumab ozogamicin. Across all of the Applicant's sponsored studies there were 34/880(4%) of hepatic VOD after treatment with inotuzumab ozogamicin. The frequency of VOD across the various studies is displayed in the table below.

L1148: Source: FDA Analysis
L1149: In Study 1022, of the 22 cases of VOD reported by Investigators in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm 5/164(3%) occurred on or after study without an intervening HSCT. The rate was lower in patients who did not have pre-study HSCT compared to those who had pre-study HSCT (2.2% vs 6.9%, respectively). A total of 17/77(22%) of VOD occurred after follow-up HSCT. The VOD (occurring post-HSCT) was lower in patients who did not have pre-study HSCT compared with those who had at least 1 prior pre-study HSCT (18% versus 45%), respectively).
L1150: In Study 1010, there were 4 patients (6%) who developed VOD and none of these patients received a pre-study HSCT. All 4 of these cases occurred in the Phase 2 Dose expansion cohort (N=48). In the Investigator Initiated Studies, there were a total of 15 VOD events and in the compassionate use of inotuzumab there were a total of 4 VOD events reported.
L1151: *Reviewer Comment: The majority of VOD cases occurred after follow-up HSCT. Monitoring of patients closely for signs and symptoms of VOD/SOS is paramount. Monitoring should include assessments of liver tests and if development of abnormal liver tests, more frequent monitoring.*
L1152: There were 3 cases of VOD that occurred in patients on Study 1022 after the March 8, 2016 database lock. The Applicant did not include these 3 cases in any summary of clinical safety tables or listings. Two cases were in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and one case was in the control arm.
L1153: ## Deaths due to VOD
L1154: In study 1022 there were five patients who were reported to have grade 5 events of VOD. All 5 of these fatal (grade 5 AE) cases occurred after follow-up HSCT. For the 17 patients who developed VOD after HSCT, there were also 6 VOD events ongoing at the time of death due to other causes (ARDS and multi-organ failure, ischemic cardiac disease and myocardial infarction).
L1155: *Reviewer Comment: VOD with multi-organ failure has mortality rate in excess of 80%. Out of these 17 patients a total of 11 patients died. Five of the deaths were considered grade 5 AEs however 4 patients had MOF as cause of death with ongoing VOD at time of death. It is likely*
L1156: *that cause of death was really attributable to VOD leading to multi-organ failure.*
L1157: ## Study 1010:
L1158: There was one death reported to have grade 5 hepatotoxicity due to VOD
L1159: ## Timing of VOD: VOD occurring before post-study HSCT or no HSCT occurred
L1160: In Study 1022, there were 5/164(3%) patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm who developed VOD during the study treatment or during the follow-up. Four of these events occurred on study and one occurred on day 57 post study. No patient in the investigator choice arm developed VOD within 42 days after the last dose of inotuzumab.
L1161: In study 1010, VOD (on or after study without follow-up HSCT) occurred in 2/72(3%) of patients. Overall of the 236 patients in the ALL studies (1022 and 1010) VOD was reported in 7(3%) of patients who received inotuzumab ozogamicin without intervening HSCT.
L1162: ## Timing of VOD: VOD occurring post-study HSCT
L1163: In Study 1022, 17/77(22%) of the patients who proceeded to HSCT after study therapy developed VOD/SOS. Five of these patients had a pre-study HSCT. Five of these 17 VOD cases had fatal outcomes, six events resolved and 6 VOD events were ongoing at time of death due to other causes. In the control arm 1/32(3%) who proceeded to HSCT, developed VOD after HSCT.
L1164: In Study 1010, VOD after follow-up HSCT was reported in 2/24 patient (8%). One of these patients died from VOD. Overall in studies 1022 and 1010, there were 101 patients with follow-up HSCT and 19 patients (19%) who received inotuzumab developed VOD after HSCT. In the NHL studies, 4 were patients proceeded to transplantation. In the single agent NHL studies, no VOD occurred HSCT and in the combination studies with rituximab or chemotherapy, 3 patients reported to have VOD after conditioning regimen and or autologous or allogeneic HSCT.

L1188: The univariate analysis suggests that last bilirubin prior to follow-up HSCT( ≥ ULN), dual alkylators, Busulfan containing regimens, prior SCT, age &gt;55, prior history of liver disease and
L1189: number of study treatment cycles are all risk factors for the development of VOD.
L1190: **Reviewer Comment:** This reviewer recommends box warning for VOD on the USPI for inotuzumab ozogamicin. In addition, section 5 of the USPI, should include the risk factors that are associated with increased risk for development of VOD. Providing the treating physician with the risk factors that increase the likelihood of developing VOD in patients who proceed to transplantation may help to impact the decision as to whether to treat a patient with inotuzumab ozogamicin.
L1191: The benefit/risk of administering inotuzumab ozogamicin who have prior history of VOD or ongoing liver disease needs to be carefully weighed in treatment decision. In addition, patients who are older, later lines of salvage or had prior HSCT and prior history of liver disease additional caution should be undertaken. Patients who undergo HSCT should avoid conditioning regimens that contain 2 alkylators and patients who receive inotuzumab who are proceeding to subsequent transplantation should limit number of cycles to 2 of inotuzumab ozogamicin.
L1192: The duration of treatment variable (interval from last dose to first date of conditioning regimen) in the univariate analysis did not appear to be as strong of risk factor for development of VOD. Prior experience with gemtuzumab ozogamicin suggested that the interval between last dose of gemtuzumab ozogamicin and start of HSCT should be 4-6 weeks. It is not known what the optimal interval is between last dose of inotuzumab and start of preparatory regimen for HSCT. No definitive recommendations can be made about time interval in the USPI, can just include the interval time and range.
L1193: ## VOD Prophylaxis and Treatment
L1194: The treatment of suspected cases of VOD included standard supportive care. Ursodeoxycholic acid and defibrotide were used per Investigator's discretion. Ursodeoxycholic acid use was reported in 78/164(48%) and 41/143(29%) of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin and investigator choice arm, respectively.

L1208: ## Adjudication of Hepatic Events
L1209: All investigators were required to submit severe hepatotoxicity forms (SHTF) for potential hepatic events to the HEAB. Based on the database cut-off date of March 8, 2016, 64 cases from 54 patients were adjudicated from the inotuzumab arm and 25 cases (25 patients) from the control arm. The HEAB adjudicated 24 events from Study 1022 as considered possible, probable or definite VOD/SOS of which 23 were from the inotuzumab arm and confirmed 1 event of grade 3 VOD in the control arm.

L1213: The investigators in Study 1022, identified 22 total VOD events and the HEAB assessed 19/22 as possible, probably or definite VOD. The remaining 3 were assessed by the HEAB as intrahepatic cholestasis and 2 due to fatal sepsis and not VOD. There were 4 additional events assessed as at least possible VOD by the HEAB but not by investigators.
L1214: *Reviewer Comment: Most of the hepatic cases adjudicated by the HEAB as VOD were moderate or severe. The mortality of VOD with multi-organ failure is over 80%. Mitigation strategies and appropriate patient selection will be important clinical considerations in choosing whether to treat a patient with inotuzumab ozogamicin who may subsequently undergo follow-up HSCT.*
L1215: Of the 4 events assessed as least possible VOD by the HEAB, one event was in a patient during study therapy. For patient 10961001, the investigator reported hyperbilirubinemia due to extrahepatic biliary obstruction and recurrent necrotizing pancreatitis while HEAB decision was extrahepatic c biliary obstruction and possible(moderate VOD).
L1216: The other three cases adjudicated by HEAB as possible VOD occurred after HSCT: patient 12721001: reported grade 2 ascites by the Investigator, but considered to be possible VOD by the HEAB, patient 12721003 reported grade 2 ascites by the investigator but considered to be possible VOD by the HEAB and patient 11671102 was reported as multi-organ dysfunction due to HHV6 infection by the investigator but was considered to represent probable VOD/SOS by the HEAB.
L1217: *Reviewer Comment: Of the 22 events assessed by Investigators in Study 1022 as VOD, the HEAB assessed 19/22 as possible (4 patients), probable (8 patients) or definite (7 patients) with VOD. The remaining 3 cases were assessed as not consistent with VOD per the HEAB. The HEAB assessed 4 additional events as possible VOD (one event during study therapy and 3 after follow-up HSCT). Overall, the investigator assessment of 22 cases of VOD and overall the HEAB assessment of VOD of 23 cases of VOD are comparable. The difference in HEAB assessments and investigator does not have impact on assessment of frequency of VOD.*
L1218: *The most significant risk of inotuzumab ozogamicin is VOD. Mild or moderate VOD usually has self-limiting course and this study was not designed to adequately capture the severity of the VOD. When VOD occurs with multi-organ failure the mortality rate is over 80%(Coppell et al). The clinical criteria used to defined the severity of disease are based upon the clinical course and can only be applied retrospectively and in a study by McDonald et. al(1993) Day + 100 survival was 91% with mild VOD and 77% with moderate disease.*
L1219: ## Infusion reactions

There was one patient in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm with a grade 3 infusion-related adverse event of pyrexia and rash maculopapular. In the control arm 7 patients (5%) had ≥ Grade 3 infusion-related reaction adverse events.

L1226: ## **Infusion Related Reactions (Cluster term) during Cycle 1**
L1227: Overall there were 44 patients with infusion related reactions (per the cluster term) in the inotuzumab arm during Cycle 1. Thirty-two patients had infusion related reaction occurring on Day 1 or day 2, eleven patients on day 7-9 and 8 patients on days 14, 15, 16 or 17. Some patients had infusion related reactions on multiple days of infusion regimen.
L1228: During Cycle 1, there were a total of 28 patients with grade 1 adverse events during all of cycle 1 however eight of these patients had treatment given. There were a total of 18 patients with grade 2 adverse events during cycle 2. There were 12 patients with infusion related reactions in Cycle 2 and eight patients with infusion related reactions in Cycle 3 or 4.
L1229: The Applicant also identified AEs considered related to infusion reactions recorded by the investigator on a separate CRF specifically designed to capture only these events. The Applicant stated and FDA confirmed that there 17 patients considered to have TEAEs related to an infusion reaction in the inotuzumab arm. Sixteen of these events occurred during Cycle 1. None of the events were grade 3 or higher. In the control arm, 23 patients were considered to have AEs related to an infusion reaction. There were no grade 4 or 5 events reported.
L1230: *Reviewer Comment: The incidence of infusion related reactions occurred most often during the first cycle with most occurring on day 1 or day 2 of the infusion. The infusion related reactions were manageable and did not prevent subsequent dosing following administration of standard precautions. Premedication with a corticosteroid, antipyretic and antihistamine are recommended prior to dosing and patients should be monitored closely during and for at least 1 hour after end of infusion for potential onset of infusion-related reactions*

L1244: Source: FDA Analysis TEAE dataset
L1245: Overall infections were reported in 49% (80/164) of patients receiving inotuzumab ozogamicin and in 77% (110/143) of patients in the investigator arm. Grade &gt; 3 infections were reported in 29% (47/164) of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 55% (79/143) patients in the
L1246: investigator choice arm. The most common infections in the inotuzumab arm were pneumonia, sepsis and bacteremia related events.
L1247: *Reviewer Comment: There were more infectious adverse events in the investigator choice arm even though treatment duration was longer in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. The pattern of infections(pneumonia, sepsis and bacteremia) are not unexpected for this heavily pretreated population.*
L1248: Complications associated with hemorrhage occurred in 55/164 (34%) of patients in the inotuzumab arm and 42/164 (29%) patients in the investigator choice arm. The most common preferred terms in the inotuzumab arm leading to hemorrhage (cluster term) included epistaxis (24/164, 15%), GI Bleed (grouped term)( 13/164, 8%) contusion(10/164,6%). In the investigator choice arm, the most common causes for hemorrhage included epistaxis 12/143(8%), petechiae (5/143, 4%) and mouth hemorrhage (5/143,5%).
L1249: During the inotuzumab therapy, 6 patients had grade 3 events(2 epistaxis and 1 each of hematuria, GI hemorrhage, gastritis hemorrhage and upper GI hemorrhage) 2 patients had Grade 4 event(intracranial hemorrhage and mesenteric hemorrhage) and 2 patients had three Grade 5 events(GI hemorrhage, intra-abdominal hemorrhage and shock hemorrhage). There were 2 grade 5 hemorrhagic events in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 1 grade 5 event in the control arm.
L1250: The grouped term for GI bleed from the cluster terms includes: rectal hemorrhage, Gastrointestinal hemorrhage, hematochezia, gastritis hemorrhagic, hematemesis, intra-abdominal hemorrhage, mesenteric hemorrhage, upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage, hemoptysis and melena.
L1251: *Reviewer Comment: Epistaxis was the most frequently reported hemorrhage AE in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. The two events of fatal (grade 5) hemorrhagic events occurred in setting of disease progression.*
L1252: ## Tumor lysis syndrome (TLS)
L1253: The incidence of tumor lysis in Study 1022 was low with 4 patients (2%) in the inotuzumab arm and 3 patients (2%) patients in the control arm having tumor lysis. Two grade 3 events and one grade 4 event occurred in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm.
L1254: *Reviewer Comment: The incidence of TLS was low. Monitor patients for TLS per standard medical practice.*
