L1263: ## 7.4.7. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1264: No studies or trials were conducted to evaluate a specific safety concern.
L1265: ## 7.4.8. Additional Safety Explorations
L1266: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1267: Neoplasms (identified using the Neoplasms, Benign, Malignant and Unspecified SOC) were rare on Study 1022. There were three patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm (2%) who developed neoplasms. Two were related to underlying malignancy (leukemia cutis and chloroma) and the other case was benign.
L1268: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1269: Study 1022 and Study 1010 excluded patients who were less than 18 years of age. The safety of inotuzumab ozogamicin in pediatric patients has not been established.
L1270: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1271: There was no experience of overdose reported in the clinical studies of inotuzumab ozogamicin in patients with relapsed or refractory ALL. In the NHL study 1004 (inotuzumab ozogamicin plus rituximab) one patient with NHL accidentally received inotuzumab ozogamicin at a dose of 2.4\text{mg}/\text{m}^2 rather than 1.8\text{mg}/\text{m}^2 during cycle 1. The reported AE for this patient included Grade 3 thrombocytopenia and grade 1 AEs (vomiting, diarrhea, hypokalemia, cough, oral herpes, and muscle spasms).
L1272: Patients do not self-administer inotuzumab ozogamicin and access to the drug is restricted to healthcare professionals and prescribed by specialists in Hematology and Oncology. There is no evidence observed that inotuzumab ozogamicin produces physical or psychological dependence in patient with hematologic malignancies.
L1273: ## 7.4.9. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1274: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1275: Inotuzumab ozogamicin is not marketed in any country. There are no post-market safety data.
L1276: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1277: ## 7.4.10. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1278: A total of 880 (73%) patients have received at least 1 dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin and for the purpose of this integrated assessment of safety only the ALL studies with single-agent
L1279: inotuzumab ozogamicin (Studies 1022 and Study 1010) are discussed in further detail.
L1280: In study 1022, 164 patients received inotuzumab and 143 patients received Investigator's choice of chemotherapy (control arm). The 164 patients received inotuzumab ozogamicin at the proposed dose. Data from the 164 inotuzumab ozogamicin treated patients in Study 1022 were pooled with data from 48/72 patients from Study 1010 given similar dosing regimens (POOLED ALL studies, n=212).
L1281: In Study 1022, the median duration of treatment in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was 8.9 weeks (median 3 cycles) and 0.9 weeks (median 1 cycle) for patients who received the control therapy. In study 1010, the median duration of treatment was 10.1 weeks (median 3 cycles). In the pooled single agent ALL studies, the median duration of treatment with inotuzumab ozogamicin was 9.1 weeks (median 3 cycles).
L1282: Common TEAEs (all reported AEs for up to 42 days after the last dose of study drug as well as all VOD/SOS during study treatment and follow-up period for up to 2 years).
L1283: - The most common (\ge 20\%) all causality TEAEs for inotuzumab ozogamicin versus control therapy were thrombocytopenia (49% vs 61%), neutropenia (49% vs 46%), anemia (34% vs 55%), nausea (32% vs 48%), pyrexia (32% vs 42%), leukopenia (29% vs 38%), headache (27% vs 27%), febrile neutropenia (27% vs 54%), fatigue (26% vs 17%), AST increase (23% vs 11%), GGT increase (21% vs 8%) and hyperbilirubinemia (21% vs 17%).
- TEAEs with frequency \ge 10\% in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the control arm (\ge 5\% absolute difference) were fatigue (26% vs 17%), AST increase (23% vs 11%), GGT increase (21% vs 8%), epistaxis (26% vs 8%), blood ALP increase 13% vs 7%), and VOD (13% vs 0.7%).
- In the pooled ALL studies, the most commonly reported AEs were thrombocytopenia (49%) and neutropenia (43%), which are similar to AEs in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm in Study 1022.
L1284: ## Grade 3 AEs
L1285: - In Study 1022, the most common (\ge 10\%) Grade 3 TEAEs for inotuzumab ozogamicin versus control therapy were neutropenia (47% vs 44%), thrombocytopenia (41% vs 59%), febrile neutropenia (27% vs 54%), leukopenia (27% vs 37%), anemia (23% vs 44%), lymphopenia (17% vs 25%), GGT increase (11% vs 5%) and VOD (11% vs 0.7%).
- In the POOLED ALL studies, the most frequently reported grade \ge 3 AEs were thrombocytopenia (42%) and neutropenia (42%) and VOD (10%). The other \ge Grade 3 were similar to the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm in study 1022.
L1286: ## SAEs
L1287: - The most common (\ge 2\%) serious adverse events for inotuzumab ozogamicin vs control therapy were VOD (13% vs &lt;1%), febrile neutropenia (12% vs 19%), pneumonia (6% vs 0%), pyrexia (3% vs 2%), and sepsis (2% vs 7%).
- In the pooled ALL studies, the most frequently reported SAEs (\ge 5\%) were febrile
L1288: neutropenia(15%), VOD(12%), pneumonia(7%) and disease progression(6%).
L1289: ## Permanent Treatment Discontinuations/Treatment Delays
L1290: - In Study 1022, TEAES associated with permanent treatment discontinuations for the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was 18% compared to 8% in the control arm with pneumonia (3%) and thrombocytopenia (2%) as the most common for the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm.
- In Study 1022, AEs associated with treatment delays were reported in 44% of patients in the inotuzumab arm compared to 12% in the control arm. The most frequently reported AE (\ge 5\%) associated with treatment delay in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was neutropenia (17%) and thrombocytopenia(10%). In the control arm there were no AE reported with frequency \ge 5\% associated with treatment delays.
- In the pooled ALL studies of single agent inotuzumab ozogamicin, the types of AEs associated with permanent discontinuations, treatment delays and dose reductions were similar to those reported in the inotuzumab ozogamicin treated patients in Study 1022.
L1291: ## Deaths
L1292: - In study 1022, grade 5 SAEs were reported in 26/164(16%) of patients in the inotuzumab arm and 16/143(11%) patients in the control arm. The most frequently reported causes of death in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus the control arm were adverse events in the SOC of General Disorders and Administration Site conditions (6% vs 5%- disease progression most frequent) and infections and infestations(5% in both arms of study). Six of the 26 deaths in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm occurred after subsequent HSCT (5 patients due to VOD and one due to multi-organ failure in setting of VOD).
- In the pooled ALL studies, Grade 5 SAEs were reported in 33(16% of patients).
- All deaths reported in the safety population includes all deaths whether during study or in follow-up period. There were 122(74%) deaths in the inotuzumab arm versus 121 deaths (85%) in the control arm. Disease under study (progressive disease during study) occurred in 75(46%) of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to 95(66%) in the control arm.
- In the Inotuzumab ozogamicin arm 77/164(47%) patients proceeded to follow-up transplantation and 33/143(20%) of patients in the control arm proceeded to transplantation.
- Post-transplantation mortality was 46/77(60%) in the inotuzumab arm and 19/33(58%) in the control arm.
- Non-relapsed mortality included death as due to other reasons other than disease under study and included patients who had relapsed or disease progression but subsequently died from causes other than disease under study. The number of patients with post-transplant non-relapse mortality was 29/77(38%) in the inotuzumab arm and 8/33(24%) in the control arm. In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm more patients died due to reasons other than disease under study (infection and VOD)There were 5 fatal VOD cases after subsequent transplantation in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 6 cases of VOD ongoing at the time of death
L1293: (death due to other mechanism) after HSCT.
L1294: ## Hepatotoxicity
L1295: - Treatment emergent adverse hepatotoxicity events were reported in 51% of patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and 34% in the control arm (respectively). The most frequently reported hepatotoxicity TEAE in the inotuzumab arm compared to the control arm were AST increase (23% vs 11%), GGT increase (21% vs 8%), hyperbilirubinemia (21% vs 17%) and ALT increase (15% vs 13%).
L1296: ## VOD
L1297: - Twenty-two patients(22/164, 13%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm had VOD during or following treatment or following HSCT after completion of treatment and in the control arm VOD was reported in 1/143(&lt;1%) of patients. Five patients had fatal VOD that occurred post-HSCT.
- Of the 22 VOD events in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, 5/164(3%) occurred during study therapy or in follow-up without intervening HSCT and 17 occurred in 77 patients (22%) after a subsequent HSCT. Five of the 22 VOD events were fatal (all post-HSCT), 7 patients recovered from VOD (1 without intervening HSCT and 6 post-HSCT) and VOD was ongoing at the time of death for 10 patients.
- Univariate analysis of risk factors for VOD indicated that use of last bilirubin prior to follow-up HSCT (\ge ULN), dual alkylators, Busulfan containing regimens, prior SCT, age \ge 55, history of liver disease and number of study treatment cycles.
- In Study 1010, the VOD total was reported in 4/72 patients(6%) and in the pooled ALL studies VOD total was reported in 26/212(12%) of patients. In the ALL studies 1022 and 1010, there were 101 patients who went to HSCT after inotuzumab ozogamicin and VOD was reported in 19/101(19%) of patients. In the single agent NHL studies VOD was reported in 1/173 patients (&lt; 1%).
- The HEAB adjudicated 19/22 cases in the inotuzumab arm as possible/probable or definite VOD/SOS. In addition, the HEAB adjudicated 4 additional cases as possible/probable or definite VOD for a total of 23 cases of VOD adjudicated by the HEAB compared to 22 cases by Investigators.
L1298: ***In summary***, the safety profile for inotuzumab ozogamicin is manageable for myelosuppression, infections and hemorrhage. Common adverse events (\ge 20%) included thrombocytopenia, neutropenia infection, anemia, leukopenia, fatigue, hemorrhage, pyrexia, nausea, headache, febrile neutropenia, transaminase increased, abdominal pain, hyperbilirubinemia and gamma-glutamyltransferase increased. There was less febrile neutropenia and severe infections in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the defined investigator choice of chemotherapy however there were more hepatic adverse events (51%) to include VOD in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. The most frequent serious adverse event in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was VOD(14%) with VOD occurring more frequently in patients who proceeded to subsequent HSCT(18/79,23%). Prior HSCT, the use of HSCT conditioning regimens that contains 2 alkylating agents, and last total bilirubin level \ge upper limit of normal before follow-up HSCT are associated with an increased risk of VOD after HSCT. Other risk factors include increased age, greater number of treatment cycles, busulfan containing regimens, later lines of salvage
L1299: therapy and history of liver disease prior to treatment. The high frequency of VOD hepatotoxicity to include VOD warrants a box warning. Mitigation strategies for VOD to include careful consideration for patient selection based on risk factors, limiting number of treatment cycles, close monitoring of patients for VOD described in the prescribing information.
L1300: ## 120 Day Safety Update
L1301: The Applicant submitted 120 day safety update on February 24, 2017. There were now a total of 79 patients who proceeded to subsequent transplant in the inotuzumab ozogamicin compared to 77. VOD was reported in 14/164(14%) rather than 13 patients. Among the 79 patients who proceeded to subsequent HSCT, VOD was reported in 18/79(23%) compared to 17/77(22%). Five of the 18 VOD events that occurred post-HSCT were fatal. VOD was reported in 13/68(19%) of patients who only received a HSCT after inotuzumab ozogamicin; an increase of one patient (was previously 12/66, 18%).
L1302: The Applicant conducted a univariate analysis based on the 79 patients who proceeded to subsequent HSCT. This reviewer confirmed the results of the univariate analysis for the 79 patients (refer to section 7.5.1). For patients who proceed to subsequent HSCT, the use of conditioning regimens that have 2 alkylating agents, last total bilirubin &gt; upper limit of normal (ULN) before follow-up HSCT are associated with significant risk in VOD after HSCT.
L1303: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1304: ## 7.5. Statistical Issues
L1305: In Study B1931022, Intozumab ozogamicin demonstrated a statically significant improvement in the ORR primary endpoint compared to the defined physicians choice of treatment. This improvement was consistent across all pre-specified stratification subgroups. However, the study failed to demonstrate a statistically significant improvement in OS based on the pre-specified criteria in the protocol.
L1306: In addition, we observed the following statistical issues:
L1307: - Log rank test is most powerful under the assumption of proportional hazard. However, the OS results show that the hazards in the two treatment arms were not proportional. Violation of proportional hazard does not necessarily make the log rank test invalid, but may make the estimated treatment effect difficult to interpret. Log rank test and median of survival may not be the optimal statistics to estimate the treatment effect.
- There exists imbalance in the censoring distributions between the two arms for the primary efficacy endpoint, i.e. OS, analysis. The reasons for the imbalance are unknown.
- The distribution of drug exposure between the intozumab ozogamicin arm and the control arm is imbalanced.
L1308: - The distribution of anti-cancer therapies between the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm and the control arm is imbalanced.
- There were more patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm who received HSCT (43% in inotuzumab ozogamicin vs. 11% in control)
- The OS result was confounded by HSCT. The interaction between HSCT and treatment in the Cox model is nominally significant (nominal p&lt;0.05).There was no difference seen between the two treatments in subjects who did not receive HSCT
- For those subjects receiving HSCT, there were fewer early deaths in the control arm, but the inotuzumab ozogamicin subjects have a better survival outcome than the control subjects after 18 months.
L1309: In our exploratory competing risk analysis, when we consider death attributed only to disease, there are fewer cumulative deaths over time in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm than the control arm. However, when we consider death attributed to causes other than disease, the trend was reversed.
L1310: ## 7.6. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1311: Inotuzumab Ozogamicin is a CD22 targeted antibody-drug conjugate comprised of a humanized immunoglobulin IgG4 covalently attached via a linker to the small molecule, N-acetyl-gamma-calicheamicin. The majority of patients (&gt;90%) with B-cell acute lymphoblastic leukemia express CD22 on the surface of malignant B-lymphocyte blast cells. The benefit-risk assessment supports standard approval of inotuzumab ozogamicin for the treatment of adults with relapsed or refractory B-cell precursor acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL).
L1312: The efficacy of inotuzumab ozogamicin is primarily based on the results of single large randomized, open-label controlled phase 3 study that compared inotuzumab ozogamicin, administered in split doses to defined investigator's choice of chemotherapy (control arm) in patients greater than or equal to 18 years of age with relapsed or refractory B-cell ALL. The primary endpoint of CR/CRi in the first 218 patients randomized who received inotuzumab was 81% (95% CI: 72%, 88%) compared to the patients who received a defined investigator's choice of chemotherapy 29% (95% CI: 21%, 39%).
L1313: The secondary endpoints of CR and duration of response were used to inform the regulatory decision making process. CR was achieved by 39 (36%) subjects (95% CI: 27%, 46%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus 19 (17%) subjects (95% CI: 11%, 26%) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. The response was durable with estimated median duration of complete response of 8 months (95% CI: 5, 10 months) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm
L1314: compared to 5 months (95% CI: 3, 7 months) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. This CR rate for single agent inotuzumab in the intended population is clearly higher than reported for any other single chemotherapeutic agents in the literature. Additional support for the conclusion of effectiveness is based on the attainment of MRD levels less than &lt;1 \times 10^{-4} as MRD levels less than &lt;1 \times 10^{-4} are expected to be reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit in the intended population. The conclusion of effectiveness is strengthened by the finding that 35/39(89.7%) of the patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm not only had a CR but also had a reduction in minimal residual disease (MRD) to &lt;1 \times 10^{-4} compared to 6/19(31.6%) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. The responses were consistent in analysis of the full intent-to-treat (ITT) population and consistent across the subpopulations tested. Additional support for the conclusion of effectiveness is strengthened by the finding that 78% (95% CI: 68%, 87%) of the patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm not only had a CR/CRi but a reduction in minimal residual disease (MRD) to &lt;1 \times 10^{-4} compared to 38% (95% CI: 24%, 47%) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm in the ITT(N=326) population.
L1315: In the inotuzumab arm, the patients with CRi appear to be comparable to patients who attained CR with regard to MRD status and durability of response. The CRi responders performed better overall with respect to MRD negativity and duration of response compared to patients who attained CRi in the chemotherapy control arm. In the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm, the rate of attainment of CRh(29%) in the ITT218 population per EAC is comparable to the rate of CR(35.8%) and CRi(45.0%). The magnitude of difference between the attainment of CRh in the inotuzumab arm (29%) compared to the investigator choice arm(5%) is notable as is the rate of MRD negativity for the patients with CRh(71.8%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus 20% in the investigator choice arm.
L1316: An improvement in overall survival for the full ITT population (N=326) did not reach statistical significance in the pivotal phase 3 study (B1931022) as it exceeded the prespecified 1-sided p-value boundary of 0.0104 in the pivotal phase 3 study(B1931022). The estimated hazard ratio for the primary endpoint of overall survival (OS) in the ITT population was 0.770(97.5% CI: 0.578, 1.026) with an estimated median OS of 7.7 months (95% CI 6, 9.2) for inotuzumab ozogamicin versus 6.7 months (95% CI 4.9 8.3) in the control arm. In the competing risk analysis when death was attributed to disease only, there are fewer cumulative deaths over time in the inotuzumab arm than the control arm. When considering deaths attributed to causes other than disease, the reverse was true, there were more cumulative deaths over time in the inotuzumab arm compared to the control arm (non-relapse mortality in inotuzumab arm of 38%(29/77) versus 24%(8/33) in the control arm). Hepatic VOD was the main contributing factor leading to the increase in non-relapse mortality in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. The increase risk of non-relapse mortality in patients with relapsed or refractory ALL who receive inotuzumab ozogamicin and proceed to subsequent HSCT supports a boxed warning in the product label.
L1317: The safety database for inotuzumab ozogamicin consists of 880 patients exposed to
L1318: inotuzumab ozogamicin with the primary assessment for safety from 2 studies (B1931022 and B1931010) in patients with relapsed or refractory ALL. In general, the safety profile for inotuzumab ozogamicin is manageable for myelosuppression, infections and hemorrhage. Common adverse events (\ge 20\%) included thrombocytopenia, neutropenia infection, anemia, leukopenia, fatigue, hemorrhage, pyrexia, nausea, headache, febrile neutropenia, increased transaminases, abdominal pain, hyperbilirubinemia and increased gamma-glutamyltransferase. There was less febrile neutropenia and severe infections in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the defined investigator choice of chemotherapy however there were more hepatic adverse events (51%) to include VOD in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm.
L1319: The most frequent serious adverse event in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm was VOD(14%) with VOD occurring more frequently in patients who proceeded to subsequent HSCT(17/77,22%). The risk factors, prior HSCT, the use of HSCT conditioning regimens that contains 2 alkylating agents, and last total bilirubin level \ge upper limit of normal before follow-up HSCT, are associated with an increased risk of hepatic VOD after HSCT. Other risk factors include increased age, greater number of treatment cycles, busulfan containing regimens, later lines of salvage therapy and history of liver disease prior to treatment. The major safety risk of VOD and post-transplantation non-relapse mortality warrant a boxed warning in the USPI. Mitigation strategies for VOD to be described in the prescribing information include careful consideration for patient selection for administration of inotuzumab ozogamicin based on risk factors, limiting number of treatment cycles, and close monitoring of patients for development of VOD.
L1320: ***In summary***, the current standard of care for the treatment of relapsed or refractory ALL is intensive combination chemotherapy and the results with this approach continue to remain disappointing. Effective new treatments are needed for patients with relapsed or refractory ALL. The conclusion on the substantial evidence of effectiveness is founded on the CR rate of 36% and durable response(median 8 months) which were substantially better than the responses for the control arm of defined investigator choices of single agent or combination chemotherapy regimens. Moreover, an MRD response was noted in 89.7% of patients who attained a CR compared to only 31.6% in the defined investigator choice chemotherapy arm. Taken together, the efficacy outcomes (CR, durability and MRD negativity) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to the control arm form a strong basis for a conclusion of effectiveness for inotuzumab ozogamicin. Although inotuzumab ozogamicin failed to demonstrate statistical significance for overall survival, patients in the inotuzumab arm who proceeded to subsequent transplantation had better survival outcomes than patients in the control arm after 18 months. The failure of the attainment of CR to translate into an improvement in overall survival that was statistically significant was potentially due to more early deaths and post-transplantation non-relapse mortality in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. Hepatic VOD was the main contributing factor to the non-relapse mortality rate. In general, the safety profile for inotuzumab ozogamicin is manageable for myelosuppression, infections and hemorrhage. The most significant risks of inotuzumab ozogamicin are VOD and post-transplant non-relapse mortality which can be adequately understood and managed by hematologists-
L1321: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761040}
## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}
L1322: oncologists who treat patients with acute leukemia, and are acceptable for a population with serious and life-threatening condition in the context of the efficacy. Overall, the risk-benefit assessment of inotuzumab ozogamicin is favorable for the treatment of patients with relapsed or refractory precursor B-cell ALL. I recommend full approval of inotuzumab ozogamicin for adult patients with relapsed or refractory B-cell ALL.

L1380: ## 13.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1381: ## 13.4.1. Exposure Response
L1382: The applicant conducted exposure-response analysis for efficacy and safety for inotuzumab ozogamicin using data obtained from the two efficacy studies conducted in patients with ALL: Study B1931010 and Study B1931022 (**Table 92**). The exposure-response relationships discussed in this BLA are based on a primarily single dose pharmacokinetics. As such, conclusions based on these analyses should be interpreted with caution. The pharmacokinetic data and sampling times from the two clinical studies are summarized in **Table 93**. The PK exposure parameters investigated were observed maximum concentration in plasma (C_{MAX}), cumulative AUC in treatment cycle 1 (cAUC_{P1}), cumulative AUC (cAUC), and average concentration defined as the cumulative AUC divided by its duration (C_{AVG}).
L1383: The pharmacokinetic exposure parameters (cAUC_{P1}, cAUC, and C_{AVG}) were calculated from the final population PK model (including covariate effects) using individual empirical Bayes estimates (see 13.4.2). Exposure-response analysis was not conducted for unconjugated calicheamicin because it was below the limit of quantitation in ~ 98% of the samples.

Arm B: control (FLAG, MXN/Ara-C, or HIDAC (21-28 day cycle))	Inotuzumab ozogamicin (Arm A) = 164 randomized and treated

L1392: ## *Exposure-Efficacy Relationship*
L1393: ## CR/CRI
L1394: A binomial logistic regression model was fit to the data to predict the probability of CR/CRI with each of PK exposure parameters run individually along with the remaining covariates in Table 94 as the full covariate model. The covariates that were not statistically significant were removed from the model to yield the final model. The parameters C_{AVG} (Table 95) and cAUCP1 (Table 96) were found to be statistically significant predictors of response along with salvage number and percentage of CD22-positivity (at a significance value of 0.05). There were no
L1395: statistically significant differences among categories 1 and 2 of the ordered salvage variable, so the information was converted into dichotomous salvage variable taking values 0 (Salvage =1) or 1 (Salvage &gt;1).

L1403: ## Overall Survival (OS)
L1404: The potential relationship between inotuzumab ozogamicin exposure and OS was performed for the ALL patient population with and without censoring for post-study HSCT. The effect of CR/CRi and MRD as prognostic factors was included in the covariate analysis (stepwise selection).
L1405: Cumulative AUC was derived from the empirical Bayes estimates obtained from the population PK model and C_{AVG} (cAUC divided by the time of its duration) was chosen as the exposure metric because it represented overall exposure during the entire period of treatment.
L1406: Stepwise covariate analysis of the variables listed in (Table 97) demonstrated that C_{AVG} was a statistically significant predictor of survival affecting the base hazard. Other factors that significantly improved the survival function fit were age at baseline and BLDH.

L1413: ## Progression Free Survival (PFS)
L1414: Similar to the overall survival E-R analysis, C_{\text{AVG}} along with the remaining covariates was investigated for its effect on PFS in with and without post-study HSCT censoring. C_{\text{AVG}} appeared as a statistically significant predictor of PFS affecting the base hazard. Other factors that significantly improved the survival function fit were BMAR and BLDH. The final model of parameters estimates (**Table 99**) and effect of covariates on the survival function are summarized as follows:
L1415: \begin{aligned}S(t) &amp;= e^{-(\text{covbase}\cdot\lambda_t)^{\text{covshape}\cdot\alpha}} \\ \text{covbase} &amp;= \text{covBMAR} \cdot \text{covCAVG} \cdot \text{covBLDH} \\ \text{covBMAR} &amp;= e^{-0.0095 \cdot (\text{BMAR}-76)} \\ \text{covCAVG} &amp;= \left(\frac{\text{CAVG}}{68.59}\right)^{-0.4063} \\ \text{covBLDH} &amp;= e^{0.3831 \cdot (\log(\text{BLDH})-5.91)}\end{aligned}

L1418: ## Exposure-Safety Relationship
L1419: Logistic regression was initially performed using a full model approach (including all tested covariates), separately with 8 different exposure parameters: cAUC, log (cAUC), C_{\text{MAX}}, log(C_{\text{MAX}}), C_{\text{AVG}}, log (C_{\text{AVG}}), cAUCP1, and log (cAUCP1). The full model was reduced by eliminating covariates that are not statistically significant to yield a final model.
L1420: There was no statistically significant positive E-R relationship for Grade 3+ neutropenia, elevated ALT, elevated AST, elevated total bilirubin, investigator-reported VOD/SOS, or hepatic events (defined by a cluster term).
L1421: A statistically significant positive E-R relationship was found between Grade 3+ thrombocytopenia and cAUCP1 and between HEAB-assessed VOD/SOS and cAUCP1 (only in patients who did not undergo HSCT post inotuzumab ozogamicin treatment) (Table 100 and Figure 22).

L1425: Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-205 applicant's exposure-response analysis.
L1426: ## *Reviewer's Analysis*
L1427: The aim of this analysis is to investigate the possibility that lower doses of inotuzumab ozogamicin may provide a better balance of safety and efficacy. In this exercise, a lower dose of inotuzumab ozogamicin was simulated using the population PK model developed by the Applicant. The dose selected for simulation was 1.2 mg/m2 administered according to the schedule highlighted in **Table 9**. This dose level was chosen because it was used in estimating the PK parameters and does not include extrapolation to untested doses.
L1428: The individual subject parameters along with subject specific covariates were used to simulate the exposure parameters C_{AVG} (**Figure 23**), cAUCP1 (**Figure 25**), and cAUC for subjects included in study B1931022 and study B1931010. C_{AVG} was calculated at the end of each cycle of treatment for 1.2 mg/m2 and 1.8 mg/m2 based on the intended dosing regimen. The logistic regression model describing the relationship between C_{AVG} and CR/CRi used to predict the probability of response at each dose level (**Figure 24**). Similarly, simulated cAUCP1 for the two dose levels was used to predict the probability of VOD (**Figure 26**) based on the logistic regression model developed by the applicant.
L1429: Conceptually, this simulation exercise supports the idea that a lower dose may decrease the probability of VOD while maintaining efficacy, especially in the patient population with &gt;90% CD22+ where treatment with lower dose appears to achieve the similar probability of CR/CRi by Cycle 4 of treatment.
L1430: The predictions of safety and efficacy from this simulation exercise should be interpreted with caution. Primarily, data from the single dose trial (B1931022) preclude thorough characterization of exposure-response relationship. Additionally, confounding factors (e.g., prior treatment regimens and preparatory HSCT treatment) affecting VOD combined low incidence rate further limit the utility of this simulation exercise to recommend an alternate dosing scheme.
L1431: **Figure 23:** Simulated C_{AVG} at the end of each treatment cycle for 1.2 \text{ mg/m}^2 dose (green boxes) and 1.8 \text{ mg/m}^2 (purple boxes) for subjects included in study B1931022 and study B1931010.
L1432: **Figure 24:** Predicted probability of CR/CRi for the 1.8 \text{ mg/m}^2 and 1.2 \text{ mg/m}^2 doses at each treatment cycle. The predicted probabilities are stratified by salvage treatment and percent CD22+, both which are statistically significant covariates.
L1433: **Figure 25:** Predicted cAUCP1 for the 1.2 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 dose (left box) and the 1.8 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 dose (right box).
L1434: **Figure 26:** Predicted probability of VOD for patients proceeding to HSCT and patients not proceeding to HSCT. Error bars represent the 95% confidence interval of the model prediction.
L1435: ## 13.4.2. Population PK Analysis
L1436: ## Background
L1437: The Applicant used population PK modeling to characterize the effect of intrinsic/extrinsic factors, concomitant medications, and organ impairment on the pharmacokinetics of inotuzumab ozogamicin. The objectives of the modeling effort, according the Applicant, are:
L1438: - to develop a model that describes inotuzumab ozogamicin PK following administration of 1.2-1.8 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 administered over 2 or 3 doses given weekly to patients with relapsed
L1439: or refractory B-cell ALL or as a single dose (0.4 - 2.4 \text{ mg/m}^2) given on Day 1 or Day 2 of each cycle to patients with relapsed refractory NHL
L1440: (2) to identify potential covariates that may be important predictors of variability in inotuzumab ozogamicin distribution and elimination
L1441: ## Data
L1442: The population PK analysis was based on pooled PK data from the two studies in patients with relapsed or refractory ALL (B1931010 and B1931022) and nine studies in patients with relapsed or refractory NHL as described in Table 101.
L1443: **Table 101:** Summary of PK information from clinical studies of inotuzumab ozogamicin

L1448: ## **Base Model**
L1449: The applicant developed a population PK model to describe inotuzumab ozogamicin only. Unconjugated calicheamicin was below the limit of quantitation in 752 (98.4%) of the total 764 samples.
L1450: The inotuzumab ozogamicin PK was described using 2-compartment model with linear and time-dependent clearance components. The time dependent clearance model is:
L1451: CL = CL_1 + CL_tCL_t = CL_2 \cdot e^{(-k_{des} \cdot Time)}
L1452: Where total clearance (CL) was a sum of time-dependent clearance (CL_t) and linear clearance (CL_1), and K_{des} is the decay coefficient of the time-dependent clearance. The selected model structure improved the fit and minimized the objective function compared to 1-compartment model and 2-compartment model with only linear elimination.
L1453: The effect of disease and analytical method (ALL and HPLC-MS/MS vs NHL and ELISA) on the CL_1, CL_2, and K_{des} were investigated. Patients with ALL had lower CL_1 and K_{des} compared to patients with NHL. Hence, this model structure was carried forward.
L1454: IVV in the PK parameters was modeled using multiplicative exponential random effects. The stability of the model was improved when random effects on peripheral compartment parameters (V_2 and Q) were removed. OMEGA block structure for CL_1, V_1, and CL_2 was found to increased model stability and minimize objective function value.
L1455: Two residual errors were included in the model, where inotuzumab ozogamicin PK concentrations were analyzed using the ELISA and HPLC/MS/MS methods for NHL and ALL, respectively. Residual variability was modeled additively based on log-transformed observation data using thetazized variance-covariance matrix of the intra-individual random effects.
L1456: Inotuzumab ozogamicin PK data &lt; LLOQ was included in the final model using a likelihood-based approach where concentrations \ge LLOQ and &lt; LLOQ were treated as continuous-type and categorical-type data, respectively. Visual predictive check confirmed the superiority of the likelihood approach compared to omitting BLQ data. The eta-shrinkage was substantially lower with likelihood approach. The final parameters estimates and model performance are summarized in Table 102 and Figure 27.

L1472: The GAM analysis results included the following potential covariates selected to test for statistical significance:
L1473: - CL1: RITX, HYDR, BBSA, BALB, BBIL, BLSTPB, and CD22PPB
- CL2: BBSA and BALB
- V1: BBSA
- Kdes: SALV, BBSA, and BLSTPB
L1474: BBSA was found to be a statistically significant covariate for CL1 (power model), CL2 (power model), and V1 (linear). BLSTPB was found to be a statistically significant covariate for Kdes. The equations below describe the final model estimation of typical values of CL1, CL2, V1, and Kdes in NHL patients:
L1475: CL_1 = 0.113 \text{ L/h} \cdot (1 - 0.745 \cdot PTST_{ALL}) \cdot (BBSA/1.84 \text{ m}^2)^{1.54} \cdot (1 + 0.155 \cdot RITX) \\ CL_2 = 0.369 \text{ L/h} \cdot (BBSA/1.84 \text{ m}^2)^{1.64} \\ V_1 = 6.70 \text{ L/h} \cdot (1 + 0.774 \cdot [BBSA - 1.84 \text{ m}^2]) \\ K_{des} = 0.0337 \text{ h}^{-1} \cdot (1 - 0.860 \cdot PTST_{ALL}) \cdot (BLSTPB/5.25 \%)^{0.0401}
L1476: The final model estimation of typical values of CL_1, CL_2, V_1, and k_{des} in ALL patients are represented with the following equations:
L1477: CL_1=0.0333 \text{ L/h} \cdot (\text{BBSA}/1.84 \text{ m}^2)^{1.54}
L1478: CL_2=0.369 \text{ L/h} \cdot (\text{BBSA}/1.84 \text{ m}^2)^{1.64}
L1479: V_1=6.70 \text{ L/h} \cdot (1+0.774 \cdot [\text{BBSA}-1.84 \text{ m}^2])
L1480: k_{des}=0.00472 \text{ h}^{-1} \cdot (\text{BLSTPB}/5.25 \%)^{-0.0401}
L1481: The parameter estimates of the final covariate model are summarized in Table 106 and the visual predictive check of the final model with covariates is illustrated in Figure 28 and Figure 29.

L1486: ## Intrinsic Factors
L1487: ## Age, Gender, and Race
L1488: During GAM analyses, the effect of age on PK parameters was not significant. Gender and race (Asian vs non-Asian) were found to be correlated with BBSA (Figure 30). Therefore, inclusion for BBSA in the final model accounted for differences based on gender and race.
L1489: **Figure 30:** Relationship between gender or race (Asian vs non-Asian) and race and BSA. Both covariates were not included in the final model due to their correlation with BSA. Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-202 applicant's population PK analysis.
L1490: Final model comparisons of ETAs (on CL_1, CL_2, and V_1) show that there are no differences in the distributions between genders or races after correcting for BSA.
L1491: ## *Baseline Body Surface Area*
L1492: Increasing BBBA was correlated with an increase in CL_1, CL_2, and V_1. The value of CL_1 and CL_2 increased by approximately 36% for BBBA of 2.21 m^2 and decreased by 24% at BBBA of 1.56 m^2, relative to the typical value at 1.84 m^2. Similarly, the value of V_1 increased by 28.8% for BBBA of 2.21 m^2 and decreased by 21.6% at BBBA of 1.56 m^2, relative to the typical value at 1.84 m^2. The final model accounts for BBBA effect on final model ETAs on CL_1, CL_2, and V_1 (i.e., ETAs centered on zero) as shown in Figure 31.
L1493: ## *Renal Impairment*
L1494: The effect of renal function on the PK of inotuzumab ozogamicin was evaluated using BCCL (Table 107: Baseline CLCR in NHL and ALL patients included in the population PK analysis **Table 107**). Base model diagnostic plots showed no visible changes in inotuzumab ozogamicin PK with BCCL and this trend was reflected in the GAM analysis where BCCL was not a significant covariate on CL_1 or CL_2. The PK data set in both NHL and ALL included 4 patients with severe (BCCL 15-29 mL/min), 121 patients with moderate (BCCL 30-59 mL/min), 234 patients with mild (BCCL 60-89 mL/min), and 377 patients with normal (BCCL \ge 90 mL/min). There was no correlation between inotuzumab ozogamicin clearance estimates and the impairment of renal function (Figure 32).
L1495: ## *Hepatic Impairment*
L1496: The impact of hepatic function on inotuzumab clearance was evaluated using the NCI ODWG criteria for hepatic impairment (normal [A], mild [B1], mild [B2], moderate [C], and severe [D]) as a categorical covariate and baseline laboratory values of BALT, BAST, and BBIL as continuous covariates (**Table 108**). Base model diagnostic plots showed no visible changes with inotuzumab ozogamicin PK with baseline NCI ODWG criteria for hepatic impairment or with any of the
L1497: baseline laboratory values (Figure 33). This observed trend was reflected in the GAM analysis where hepatic function was a statistically significant covariate.
L1498: Figure 31: ETAs centered on zero for CL1, CL2, and V2 in the final model when BBSSA was included as a covariate. Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-202 applicant's population PK analysis.

## {BESPONSA; Inotuzumab Ozogamicin}

L1507: ## Extrinsic Factors
L1508: The effects of concomitant medications on inotuzumab ozogamicin PK were tested in GAM. There were varying numbers of patients taking P-glycoprotein (PGP) inhibitors (17%), granulocyte colony stimulating factor (GCSF, 22.6%), hydroxyurea (HYDR, 1.63%), inotuzumab ozogamicin given with concomitant treatment for first 2 cycles (COMB, 59.6%), prior radiotherapy (RADIO, 52.2%), and salvage therapy (SALV, Salvage 1 = 53.2%, Salvage 2 = 33.3%, and Salvage 3+ = 13.2%). For HYDR, patients were all from the ALL studies and for RITX, patients
L1509: were all from the NHL studies. GAM results showed no significant effects of PGP, GCSF, COMB, and RADIO on CL_1 and CL_2; however, SALV was significant on K_{des}, and HYDR and RITX were significant on CL_1. After testing the covariates in the stepwise covariate modeling, RITX was retained as the only significant covariate on CL_1. Hence, PGP, GCSF, HYDR, COMB, RADIO, and SALV were concluded to have no effect on inotuzumab ozogamicin PK.
L1510: In the final model, the effect of RITX (patients without Rituximab) was estimated to increase clearance by 16% (95% CI: 5.47%-26.5%). The final model did not fully account for the effects of rituximab, where ETA on CL_1 was not centered at zero (**Figure 34**). The applicant stated that the use of concomitant rituximab treatment may have some confounding factors as the NHL patients took rituximab in combination with other concomitant medications or with chemotherapy.
L1511: **Figure 34:** Rituximab treatment inclusion as a covariate in the model did not account rituximab effects on ETA. Source: report PMAR-EQDD-B193a-DP4-202 applicant's population PK analysis.
L1512: The applicant's analyses followed a reasonable and thorough approach in describing the pharmacokinetics of inotuzumab ozogamicin. The applicant's model selection and validation criteria are acceptable. To that end, the reviewer agrees with the applicant's conclusions regarding lack of effect of intrinsic (other than BSA) and extrinsic factors on the pharmacokinetics of inotuzumab ozogamicin. However, the conclusions regarding the lack of effect are only supportive in nature due to the fact that the timing and administration of concomitant medications was not included in the model.
L1513: ## 13.4.3. Bioanalytical Method Report
L1514: ## HPLC/MSMS Assay (Study B1931010 and B1931022)
L1515: ## Total Calicheamicin
L1516: In this assay, an aliquot of study sample was initially extracted with methyl tert-butyl ether (MTBE) to separate unconjugated calicheamicin from inotuzumab ozogamicin. The MTBE layer containing unconjugated calicheamicin was discarded, and the remaining aqueous layer
L1517: containing intact inotuzumab ozogamicin was fortified with internal standard (IS) N-propionyl-\gamma-calicheamicin and treated with dithiothreitol, resulting in the release of conjugated N-acetyl-\gamma-calicheamicin DMH by disulfide bond reduction and its intramolecular rearrangement to N-acetyl-\epsilon-calicheamicin (the IS converted to N-propionyl-\epsilon-calicheamicin). N-acetyl-\epsilon-calicheamicin (along with an IS) was then extracted by MTBE and measured as surrogate analyte for inotuzumab ozogamicin using reverse phase chromatography and positive ion electrospray tandem mass spectrometry. The method parameters for total calicheamicin are summarized in Table 109.
L1518: Table 109: HPLC/MSMS method parameters and validation summary of total calicheamicin

L1526: ## *Electrochemiluminescence Assay (Anti-Drug Antibody Measurement)*
L1527: The potential for immunogenicity to inotuzumab ozogamicin was measured using a bridging ECL assay. This assay was used to detect and measure the titer of serum immunoglobulins that specifically bind to inotuzumab ozogamicin (Table 111). All patient samples that tested positive in the ECL bridging assay were further tested using a cell-based nAb assay because a polyclonal antibody response to a drug product may result in both non-neutralizing and nAb (Table 112).

L1539: ## 17 Division Director (Clinical)
L1540: - I recommend approval of inotuzumab ozogamicin based on substantial evidence of effectiveness for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory precursor B-cell ALL. The Applicant conducted an adequate and well-controlled randomized phase 3 trial in patients aged 18 years of age or older with relapsed or refractory ALL. The endpoints of CR and duration of response in the first 218 randomized patients were used for the regulatory decision making process. As noted earlier, CR was achieved by 39/109 (36%) patients (95% CI; 27%, 46%) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm versus 19/109 (17%) patients (95% CI: 11%, 26%) in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. This result was statistically significant. Additionally the responses were durable with estimated median duration of CR of 8 months (95% CI: 5, 10 months) in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. The durable complete response/remission results are supported by the achievement of MRD negativity in patients who achieved CR: 35/39 (90%) in inotuzumab ozogamicin arm compared to 6/19 (32%) in investigator's choice of chemotherapy arm. Also the results were consistent with the analysis of the full intent-to-treat (ITT) population (N=326) and consistent across the subpopulations tested. The second primary endpoint was overall survival; however, an improvement in overall survival based on the ITT population was not demonstrated. Additional support for approval is the fact that more patients in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm received a potentially curative procedure (transplantation) compared with the investigator's choice arm. A competing risk analysis based on patients who proceeded to subsequent hematopoietic stem cell transplantation, showed an increase in non-relapse mortality in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm of 38% compared with 24% in the investigator's choice arm. Hepatic VOD was the main contributing factor for the increase in non-relapse mortality in the inotuzumab ozogamicin arm. A boxed warning in labeling describes risk of VOD and recommends if a patient develops VOD that the patient stop taking Besponza and initiate treatment for VOD. Besides VOD, other safety concerns were myelosuppression, infection, hemorrhage, pyrexia, and elevated liver function tests. In summary the data show a favorable risk-benefit for inotuzumab ozogamicin.
L1541: Ann. T. Farrell, M.D.

L1581: Figure 4 displays two cumulative distribution function (CDF) plots showing the predicted probability of CR/CRI response versus Inotuzumab ozogamicin Cave (ng/mL) for patients stratified by prior salvage therapy (Salvage=1 and Salvage>1) and baseline CD22-positivity (<90% and \ge90%).
The left plot (Salvage=1) shows predicted probabilities for Cycle 2 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 3 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 4 Day 1 pre-dose, and Cycle 5 Day 1 pre-dose. The right plot (Salvage>1) shows predicted probabilities for Cycle 2 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 3 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 4 Day 1 pre-dose, and Cycle 5 Day 1 pre-dose. In both plots, the predicted probability increases with Cave concentration, and higher baseline CD22-positivity (<90% vs \ge90%) generally leads to a higher predicted probability of response.
L1582: Figure 5 displays two cumulative distribution function (CDF) plots showing the predicted probability of CR/CRI response versus Cumulative Inotuzumab Exposure (AUC) on Cycle 1 (\text{mg}^2\text{hr}/\text{L}) for patients stratified by prior salvage therapy (Salvage=1 and Salvage>1) and baseline CD22-positivity (<90% and \ge90%).
The left plot (Salvage=1) shows predicted probabilities for Cycle 2 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 3 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 4 Day 1 pre-dose, and Cycle 5 Day 1 pre-dose. The right plot (Salvage>1) shows predicted probabilities for Cycle 2 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 3 Day 1 pre-dose, Cycle 4 Day 1 pre-dose, and Cycle 5 Day 1 pre-dose. In both plots, the predicted probability increases with cumulative exposure, and higher baseline CD22-positivity (<90% vs \ge90%) generally leads to a higher predicted probability of response.
L1583: Figure 6: A plot showing the predicted probability of HEAB-assessed VOD/SOS as a function of cumulative AUC after the first cycle of treatment (cAUCP1). The x-axis is Cumulative AUC After Period 1 (ng*hr/mL), ranging from 0 to 60. The y-axis is Predicted Probability, ranging from 0.0 to 0.6. The plot shows two main trends based on Post-Therapy HSCT status. For patients who Received HSCT (blue line and shaded area), the predicted probability of VOD/SOS increases significantly with cumulative AUC, reaching approximately 0.55 at 60 ng*hr/mL. For patients who Did not receive HSCT (red line and shaded area), the predicted probability remains low, around 0.15 at 60 ng*hr/mL.

The legend indicates 'CL' (Clearance) and 'median'.

The left plot (Salvage=1) shows predicted probability versus Inotuzumab ozogamicin Cave concentration (ng/mL). The right plot (Salvage>1) shows predicted probability versus Inotuzumab ozogamicin Cave concentration (ng/mL).
In both plots, the predicted probability increases with Cave concentration. The median Cave values for Day 1 pre-dose across cycles 2, 3, 4, and 5 are indicated by vertical dashed lines. The legend indicates Baseline CD22+(<90%) (Red/Pink) and Baseline CD22+ (\ge90%) (Teal/Cyan).
L1598: Figure 21 displays two cumulative distribution function (CDF) plots showing the predicted probability of CR/CRI response for Inotuzumab Ozogamicin treatment, stratified by prior salvage therapy (Salvage=1 vs. Salvage>1) and baseline CD22-positivity (<90% vs. \ge90%).
The left plot (Salvage=1) shows predicted probability versus Cumulative Inotuzumab Exposure (AUC) on Cycle 1 (\text{mg} \cdot \text{hr}/\text{L}). The right plot (Salvage>1) shows predicted probability versus Cumulative Inotuzumab Exposure (AUC) on Cycle 1 (\text{mg} \cdot \text{hr}/\text{L}).
In both plots, the predicted probability increases with cumulative exposure. The legend indicates Baseline CD22+(<90%) (Red/Pink) and Baseline CD22+ (\ge90%) (Teal/Cyan).

Top Left: Salvage = 1 and >90\% CD22+.

L1604: Figure 27 displays six plots arranged in a 2x3 grid, illustrating the performance of a population pharmacokinetic (PK) model. The top row shows Observed Concentration (ng/mL) versus Population Prediction (left) and Individual Prediction (right). The middle row shows Individual Weighted Residuals versus Time After First Dose (h) (left) and Time After Each Dose (h) (right). The bottom row shows Individual Weighted Residuals versus Population Prediction (ng/mL) (left) and Individual Prediction (ng/mL) (right). In all plots, observed data points (green circles) and predicted values (red triangles) are plotted against a diagonal line (y=x) and a horizontal line at zero residuals. The plots generally show good agreement between observed and predicted concentrations, and residuals are randomly scattered around zero, indicating a well-fitted model.

The plots show observed data points (blue dots) and predicted concentration ranges (shaded areas and lines) from the final population PK model.

Top Right Plot: Time After First Dose (h) ranges from 0 to 1000. Log Inotuzumab Concentrations (ng/mL) range from -2 to 10.

Bottom Right Plot: Time After First Dose (h) ranges from 0 to 150. Log Inotuzumab Concentrations (ng/mL) range from -2 to 10.
The VPCs illustrate the model's performance across different time scales, showing good alignment between observed and predicted concentrations.
L1606: Figure 29 displays four plots showing Log Inotuzumab Concentrations (ng/mL) versus Time After First Dose (h) for relapsed or refractory patients with NHL. The plots cover different time scales: Top Left (0 to 3000 h), Top Right (0 to 1500 h), Bottom Left (0 to 1000 h), and Bottom Right (0 to 20 h). Each plot shows individual patient data points (blue dots) and the predicted concentration profile (red solid line with dashed lines indicating confidence intervals). The concentration generally peaks around 6-7 ng/mL shortly after the first dose and then declines, with some patients showing a secondary peak around 600-800 hours.

L1608: Figure 33 displays six scatter plots arranged in a 3x2 grid, comparing the Base Model (left column) and the Final Model (right column) for the relationship between Body Surface Area (BSA, m^2) and the estimated effect (\eta) on three pharmacokinetic parameters: CL1, CL2, and V1.
The plots are labeled:
Top row: ETA on CL1
Middle row: ETA on CL2
Bottom row: ETA on V1
In all plots, the X-axis represents BSA (m^2) ranging from 1.5 to 2.5. The Y-axis represents the estimated effect (\eta) on the respective parameter, ranging from -1.0 to 2.0 (CL1 and V1) or -2.0 to 3.0 (CL2).
Each plot shows a large scatter of data points. A horizontal blue line is drawn at \eta = 0. A red dashed line represents the fitted trend line for the Base Model, and a solid red line represents the fitted trend line for the Final Model.
For ETA on CL1 and ETA on CL2, the fitted trend lines show a slight positive correlation between BSA and the estimated effect, indicating that higher BSA is associated with a slightly higher estimated effect on CL1 and CL2. The Final Model trend line is slightly steeper than the Base Model trend line for CL1.
For ETA on V1, the fitted trend lines show a slight negative correlation between BSA and the estimated effect, indicating that higher BSA is associated with a slightly lower estimated effect on V1. The Final Model trend line is slightly steeper (more negative) than the Base Model trend line for V1.
L1609: Figure 32 displays three box plots showing the estimate of Inotuzumab Ozogamicin Clearance (L/h) stratified by Renal Disease Stage (normal, mild, moderate, severe). The Y-axis represents Clearance (L/h), ranging from 0.0 to 1.0. The legend indicates CL (Clearance) and median.
The top panel shows data for ALL patients. The median clearance decreases significantly as renal disease stage worsens, from approximately 0.05 L/h in the normal stage to approximately 0.01 L/h in the severe stage.
The middle panel shows data for NHL patients. The median clearance also decreases as renal disease stage worsens, from approximately 0.05 L/h in the normal stage to approximately 0.01 L/h in the severe stage.
The bottom panel shows data for combined patients. The median clearance decreases as renal disease stage worsens, from approximately 0.05 L/h in the normal stage to approximately 0.01 L/h in the severe stage.
In all panels, the interquartile range (IQR) and overall distribution of clearance values are shown, indicating that clearance is generally lower in patients with severe renal disease compared to those with normal renal function.
L1610: Figure 33 displays three box plots showing the estimate of Inotuzumab Ozogamicin Clearance (L/h) stratified by Hepatic NCI Grade (A, B1, B2, C, D) for three patient populations: ALL patients (top panel), NHL patients (middle panel), and combined patients (bottom panel).
The Y-axis represents Inotuzumab Ozogamicin Clearance (L/h), ranging from 0.0 to 1.0. The X-axis represents Hepatic NCI Grade. The legend indicates CL (Clearance) and median.
Top Panel (ALL patients): Clearance is highest in the Normal (A) group (median around 0.15 L/h) and decreases significantly in Mild (B1) (median around 0.1 L/h), Mild (B2) (median around 0.05 L/h), and Moderate (C) (median around 0.02 L/h) groups. The Moderate (C) group shows the lowest median clearance.
Middle Panel (NHL patients): Clearance is highest in the Normal (A) group (median around 0.15 L/h) and decreases in Mild (B1) (median around 0.1 L/h), Mild (B2) (median around 0.05 L/h), and Moderate (C) (median around 0.02 L/h) groups. The Moderate (C) group shows the lowest median clearance.
Bottom Panel (Combined patients): Clearance is highest in the Normal (A) group (median around 0.15 L/h) and decreases in Mild (B1) (median around 0.1 L/h), Mild (B2) (median around 0.05 L/h), and Moderate (C) (median around 0.02 L/h) groups. The Moderate (C) group shows the lowest median clearance.
In all panels, the distribution of clearance values is generally wider in the Normal (A) group compared to the Mild (B1) and Mild (B2) groups, and the median clearance decreases as the Hepatic NCI Grade increases.
L1611: Figure 34 displays two box plots comparing the Base Model and the Final Model for ETA on CL1, stratified by whether Inotuzumab Ozogamicin was administered with Rituximab (0 or 1). In both models, the distribution of ETA on CL1 is centered around zero, suggesting no significant effect of Rituximab treatment inclusion as a covariate on ETA. The y-axis is labeled ETA on CL1, and the x-axis is labeled Inotuzumab Ozogamicin Administered with Rituximab.
